IDENTICAL IN BOTH TEXTS
### 9.4 The budget unit

Generation G uses the materialised-instruction unit and pre-instruction checks of TDL-9;
each coherent slice separately uses the reference-row unit below. R uses §17.6.

One step is one reference-interpreter row of the reference object (ISA §14.1): the wrapper of
§16.2 (including the entry-state replay of §8.5 and the shadow-stack setup of §17.3) and every
lproc row, so the fault set is a property of the program, the budget, the LLVM version and
pipeline, the normaliser and mapping versions and the runtimes version, never of the plugin's
lowering (NS §4.2). Heap bytes are counted by `lift.alloc` (`heap_used + max(n, 1)`), stack cells
by `lift.call` (R_f cells per call, ISA §3.7, monotonic), depth by `lift.call`/`lift.ret`. The
R-tier unit is §17.6.

Every oracle budget MUST retain explicit steps, heap, stack and depth components and charge wrapper, replay and helper rows under the canonical map, with shadow-stack backing charged to heap. [deps: DLT-9.1, DLT-9.4, DLT-17.3, ISA-5.4-P2, ISA-3.7]



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 17.5 Fault outcomes

| Event | Fault code (ISA §5.4) |
|---|---|
| `udiv`, `urem`, `sdiv`, `srem` with divisor 0 | 5 `FAULT_UB_DIV0` |
| `sdiv`, `srem` of the minimum value by −1 | 6 `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW` |
| an access outside every live allocation; `free` of a non-null pointer that is not the base of a live heap allocation | 7 `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` |
| `unreachable` reached; N11; an indirect call or `indirectbr` matching no candidate (§14.2, §14.9); unwinding out of a region (§14.4); a descriptor shape mismatch (`T-IFACE`); `T-TAINT-HOST`; every other run-time check of §18.3 | 4 `FAULT_UB_TRAP` |
| `__bennett_nz_fault(C)` | C |
| steps budget exhausted | 1 `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` |
| heap budget or shadow stack exhausted | 2 `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` |
| depth or lifted stack budget exhausted | 3 `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` |
| a trapped call, an unmodelled or unbound device | 8 `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED` |
| entropy exhausted (ISA §7.3; §11.3) | 10 `FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED` |
| a blocker point (§25.1) | 4, with the blocker report |

In an oracle region a fault is the F_B outcome (ISA §5.2). In an action region it is TDL-8's. In
other device code the run ends `FAILED` with that fault record (ISA §10); CC reports the code by
§11.5; for R code the required typed record depends on BLK-5/ISA-7 (§25.1);
ISA-8 (§25.2) separately governs status delivery and reverse eligibility.

The R-entry classification of §11.5 applies to all §17.6 counters and §17.5 typed
fault points; successful-path execution examples below retain their results.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
### 17.5 Fault outcomes

| Event | Fault code (ISA §5.4) |
|---|---|
| `udiv`, `urem`, `sdiv`, `srem` with divisor 0 | 5 `FAULT_UB_DIV0` |
| `sdiv`, `srem` of the minimum value by −1 | 6 `FAULT_UB_SDIV_OVERFLOW` |
| an access outside every live allocation; `free` of a non-null pointer that is not the base of a live heap allocation | 7 `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT` |
| `unreachable` reached; N11; an indirect call or `indirectbr` matching no candidate (§14.2, §14.9); unwinding out of a region (§14.4); a descriptor shape mismatch (`T-IFACE`); `T-TAINT-HOST`; every other run-time check of §18.3 | 4 `FAULT_UB_TRAP` |
| `__bennett_nz_fault(C)` | C |
| steps budget exhausted | 1 `FAULT_BUDGET_STEPS` |
| heap budget or shadow stack exhausted | 2 `FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP` |
| depth or lifted stack budget exhausted | 3 `FAULT_BUDGET_DEPTH` |
| a trapped call, an unmodelled or unbound device | 8 `FAULT_EFFECT_UNMODELLED` |
| entropy exhausted (ISA §7.3; §11.3) | 10 `FAULT_ENTROPY_EXHAUSTED` |

In an oracle region a fault is the F_B outcome (ISA §5.2). In an action region it is TDL-8's. In
other device code the run ends `FAILED` with that fault record (ISA §4.9, §10), reported by §11.5.
A `FAILED` run is reverse-eligible under ISA §10 (K6); reversal removes the record and reverses the
prefix, open activations included.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 17.6 The R tier

- The R lowering inserts a **step counter**. Before each counted materialised LLVM
  instruction, check that one step remains, then consume it before execution; otherwise
  raise code 1 before executing the instruction. Count every instruction except phi,
  debug intrinsics and N34 marks, including straight-line suffixes and returns. Batching
  is permitted only if it has the identical first-fault boundary and effect-tape prefix
  as these per-instruction checks. Heap/depth checks likewise precede mutation.
- Exhaustion is `__bennett_nz_fault(1)`, `(2)` or `(3)`, with the typed-fault contract of
  §17.5. On ISA 0.8 R sites needing this code preservation are blocked:BD-B-ISA7 (§25.1),
  not claimed implemented by a generic trap. Affected fault points stop before later effects.
- R faults end the run (ISA §4.1); §25.2 records ISA-8 (fault status to the CC caller,
  reverse-eligibility of faulted runs).

The zero-step example has the same named ISA dependency verdict as the explicit
typed-fault example below: `blocked:BD-B-ISA7` (BLK-5). This is a verdict on
ISA 0.8, not a claim that host execution fuel is the dialect's R step counter.
The materialised boundary after the failing pre-instruction check is:

```llvm example=r-budget-prefix verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA7
define internal void @budgeted(ptr %e) {
  call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 1)
  unreachable
}
define void @top() {
  call void @__bennett_reversible(ptr @budgeted, ptr null, ptr null)
  ret void
}
```

```text blocker=r-budget-prefix
BD-B-ISA7 @budgeted pre-instruction step check: BLK-5 (ISA-7); ISA 0.8 cannot report typed R fault 1; requires ISA 0.9 fault C with exact terminal record code C, no later source effect, reversible prefix history retained
```

```text state=r-budget-prefix at=0 verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA7
source R body: console.write("x"); return; steps=0
ISA 0.8 status=blocked:BD-B-ISA7 (BLK-5); generic trap is not equivalent
required under ISA 0.9: fault code=1 before first counted instruction; console=empty; tape prefix=empty
required under ISA 0.9: run=FAILED; exactly one terminal record, code=1; no host request; reversible prefix history retained
```

The exact dependency is specified in the read-only ISA 0.9 draft at commit
`61550b4`, section 0.2 row K5 and section 4.1 row `fault`: CC/R `fault C` takes
a literal 0 ≤ C < 2^64, records the exact nonzero C (zero means code 4), ends
FAILED without a successor, and preserves prefix history for reversal. The
implicit cause table remains part of BLK-5. The expected zero-step result above
requires the dialect's per-instruction counter lowered to `fault 1`; ISA 0.9
does not turn the interpreter's host-fuel option into that counter. Both this
example and `r-fault-code` are runtime/materialised IR, not producer inputs.

The next module is runtime/materialised IR (reserved primitives are not producer inputs).

```llvm example=r-fault-code verdict=blocked:BD-B-ISA7
define internal void @fail(ptr %e) {
  call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 2)
  unreachable
}
define void @top() {
  call void @__bennett_reversible(ptr @fail, ptr null, ptr null)
  ret void
}
```

```text blocker=r-fault-code
BD-B-ISA7 @fail call @__bennett_nz_fault(2): ISA 0.8 generic R trap cannot preserve C; BLK-5 (ISA-7) R fault C terminates with record code C, no later effects, reversible prefix history retained
```

```text state=r-fault-code at=1
required code=2; no subsequent source effect; prefix history intact
ISA 0.8 status=blocked:BD-B-ISA7; generic trap code is not claimed equivalent
ISA 0.9 expected: run=FAILED; exactly one terminal record code=2; no host request; no later effects; prefix history retained
```



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
### 17.6 The R tier

- The R lowering inserts a **step counter**. Before each counted materialised LLVM
  instruction, check that one step remains, then consume it before execution; otherwise
  raise code 1 before executing the instruction. Count every instruction except phi,
  debug intrinsics and N34 marks, including straight-line suffixes and returns. Batching
  is permitted only if it has the identical first-fault boundary and effect-tape prefix
  as these per-instruction checks. Heap/depth checks likewise precede mutation.
- Exhaustion is `__bennett_nz_fault(1)`, `(2)` or `(3)`, lowered to `fault 1`, `fault 2` or `fault 3`.
  R budget exhaustion MUST lower the existing pre-instruction counter check to fault 1, 2 or 3, preserve the preceding history and execute no later source effect. [deps: DLT-17.6-P1, DLT-17.6-P2, DLT-10-P37, ISA-4.1-P14, ISA-4.9-P13, ISA-10]
- R faults end the whole run (ISA §4.1, §4.9): a CC caller receives no status and runs no successor;
  the launch result carries the code (ISA §10).

The counter is dialect instrumentation; ISA 0.9 does not turn an interpreter's host-fuel option
into it (ISA §4.9 "Charge and history"). The materialised boundary after the failing pre-instruction check is:

```llvm example=r-budget-prefix verdict=accept mode=materialised lowering=K5
define internal void @budgeted(ptr %e) {
  call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 1)
  unreachable
}
define void @top() {
  call void @__bennett_reversible(ptr @budgeted, ptr null, ptr null)
  ret void
}
```

```text lowering-report=r-budget-prefix
K5 @budgeted pre-instruction step check: fault 1; terminal record, no later source effect, reversible prefix history retained
```

The witness runs the source R body `console.write("x"); return` under the §17.6 counter. Two instructions
count, so the budget boundaries are 0, 1 and 2:

```text state=r-budget-prefix at=0
steps=0 run: status=FAILED fault=1 console= tape=0 records=1
steps=0 reverse: status=REVERSED console= memory_equals_entry=true
steps=1 run: status=FAILED fault=1 console=78 tape=1 records=1
steps=1 reverse: status=REVERSED console= memory_equals_entry=true
steps=2 run: status=COMPLETED fault=none console=78 tape=1 records=0
steps=2 reverse: status=REVERSED console= memory_equals_entry=true
```

Both this example and `r-fault-code` are runtime/materialised IR, not producer inputs.

The next module is runtime/materialised IR (reserved primitives are not producer inputs).

```llvm example=r-fault-code verdict=accept mode=materialised lowering=K5
define internal void @fail(ptr %e) {
  call void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 2)
  unreachable
}
define void @top() {
  call void @__bennett_reversible(ptr @fail, ptr null, ptr null)
  ret void
}
```

```text lowering-report=r-fault-code
K5 @fail call @__bennett_nz_fault(2): fault 2; terminal record, no later source effect, reversible prefix history retained
```

The witness also runs the extreme codes, and an R call whose CC caller would fault with another code afterwards:

```text state=r-fault-code at=1
code=2 run: status=FAILED fault=2 records=1
code=18446744073709551615 run: status=FAILED fault=18446744073709551615 records=1
code=0 run: status=FAILED fault=4 records=1
caller-successor code=2 run: status=FAILED fault=2 records=1
```



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off; the null region [0,
`null_size`) with `null_size` = 4096; the data sections (`.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order)
from `data_base` = 4096; the text from `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when the device ranges below
(data, heap, arena, interface regions, computed first) end above 0x400000, from the first multiple
of 0x200000 at or after their end, so that the native comparison binary maps its code disjointly
from them; the defaults are options (`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`,
§24). The link map gives A(F) for
every function and the base of every global. The device `layout` (ISA §9.1) replicates it:

- `globals`: every global of the link at its link address with its image;
- `heap_base`: the first multiple of 4096 at or after the end of the data; `heap_limit_max`: the
  largest `heap` of the budget table (which covers the shadow stack, §9.2);
- the arena (TDL-4) at the first multiple of its size 2^A at or after the heap's end, with 2^A the
  smallest power of two covering the may-Co globals and the coherent allocator's heap (its size
  from `--bennett-arena-heap`, default the largest `heap` of the table); the arena's shadow
  `__bennett_rt_shadow` is a global of the data sections;
- interface regions per oracle above the arena (§16.3); H, A_tab and S_cells by §16.3;
- the production host image is linked with the same addresses for every function and global
  whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4).

*(non-normative)* With data low and text high, H covers the data, heap and regions; function
addresses are values only and never occupy device memory.

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
### 19.5 Layout: the comparison link

The driver links the materialised module natively (§17.7) with a linker script that fixes the
addresses: non-PIE; identical-code folding and constant merging off. The link map gives A(F)
for every function and the base of every program global. The following is the **single
range-order and non-overlap rule** for §6.5, TDL-4 and §16.3. `align_up(x,a)` is the least
multiple of positive a at or above x; all arithmetic below is mathematical, without wrapping.
All intervals are half-open.

1. Reserve the null region [0, `null_size`), default `null_size` = 4096. Lay out ordinary
   `.rodata`, `.data`, `.bss`, in that order, from `data_base` = 4096, with each object's
   target alignment. These include the arena's `__bennett_rt_shadow` when needed and exclude
   `__bennett_rt_qtab`. May-Co globals are relocated into the arena in both builds (TDL-4);
   they have no second classical global or image (§16.3). Let `data_end` be the end of
   ordinary data (or `data_base` if empty).
2. Set `heap_base = align_up(data_end,4096)` and `heap_limit_max` to the largest frozen
   `heap` capacity of the budget table (§9.2), including shadow-stack allocations. Reserve
   [heap_base, `heap_end`), where `heap_end = heap_base + heap_limit_max`.
3. If an arena exists, let its size 2^A be the smallest power of two covering the may-Co
   globals, their alignment padding and the coherent allocator's heap
   (`--bennett-arena-heap`, default the table's largest `heap`). Set
   `A_coh = align_up(heap_end,2^A)` and `coh_end = A_coh + 2^A`. Otherwise set
   `coh_end = heap_end`. An oracle's `coh` interface is exactly this arena range; it is
   one range, not an additional allocation overlapping the arena.
4. For **each** oracle shape in the complete link, start a cursor at `coh_end`. Place its
   non-`coh` interface regions in `<mems>` order: for a region of size s = 2^A, set its
   base to `align_up(cursor,s)`, then advance the cursor to base+s. Let `regions_end` be
   the maximum of `coh_end` and all these final cursors, including shapes not yet emitted.
   Different oracles MAY reuse the same interface addresses: their regions are never
   simultaneous in one oracle layout (ISA §7.2).
5. If §6.5 needs a qreg table, reserve all the ranges from steps 1–4 first, then choose
   `qtab_base`: the **least** 4096-aligned b at or above `heap_end` for which [b,b+B_tab)
   is within [0,2^pw) and intersects neither the arena nor any interface range of any
   oracle shape. Equivalently, start at `align_up(heap_end,4096)` and, whenever the
   candidate intersects a reserved range, advance to the aligned end of that range and
   repeat. This uses otherwise unallocated alignment gaps when they fit; beyond all
   reserved ranges it gives `align_up(regions_end,4096)`. Set `qtab_end = qtab_base + B_tab`. Add
   `__bennett_rt_qtab` to `layout.globals` at that base with size `B_tab` and its image
   below. Otherwise no table range or symbol exists. The table is device-private storage:
   it is excluded from ordinary data placement and the native comparison image.

For each oracle, the null region, ordinary classical globals (including shadow storage),
heap, arena/`coh`, other interface regions and private table MUST be pairwise disjoint;
empty ranges occupy no bytes. Every range MUST lie within [0,2^pw), with each end at most
2^pw, and obey the alignments above. These invariants apply to the complete device link
and after ISA linking (§9.3), before any reference-object restriction (§16.3). In particular,
the table is reserved against **every** oracle's regions, not just those in the first emitted
reference object. Steps 1–4 keep the numeric order data, heap, arena, then each oracle's
other regions; step 5 places only private storage in a free gap or after them. H, A_tab and
S_cells are then frozen by §16.3; the table adds one pinned global pre-entry and does not
consume any program heap budget. Failure to place the private table is a build-time layout
diagnostic only when no aligned gap of `B_tab` bytes remains at or above the heap end,
naming the sizes/options involved; a table/arena or table/interface combination that fits
these rules MUST be admitted without an additional
run-time fault, smaller default limits, or a restriction on program kind.

Text starts at `text_base` = 0x400000, or, when `regions_end` exceeds it, at
`align_up(regions_end,0x200000)`. Thus native code maps disjointly from ordinary data, heap,
arena and interfaces. The private table does not participate in native text placement:
function addresses are values only in device memory, so their numerical coincidence with
private device storage is harmless. The production host image uses these same addresses
for every function and global whose address may cross the boundary (§4.4). Adding a table
therefore changes no program global, heap, arena, interface or function address, nor the
native layout ABI below. The data/text defaults are options
(`--bennett-layout-data-base`, `--bennett-layout-text-base`, §24).

**Native layout ABI v1.** The driver MUST define exactly one external constant `@__bennett_rt_native_layout = constant { i64, i64, i64 } { i64 1, i64 heap_base, i64 heap_limit_max }, align 8` in comparison-only support, with fields at byte offsets 0, 8 and 16 in target little-endian order and values from this same resolved §19.5 layout; runtimes declares that object and validates the version/range before use, with no weak/default definition. The third field is a byte capacity, not an end address; it includes shadow-stack allocations under §9.2. This object has no relationship to blockaddress identity objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>`.

**Qreg table data image.** For the table placed by the rule above, the object MUST define a
data symbol named `__bennett_rt_qtab` with signature
`{"abi":"0.9","kind":"data","size":B_tab,"tier":"-"}` (substitute the resolved integer)
and `layout.globals.__bennett_rt_qtab = {"base":qtab_base,"image":"__bennett_rt_qtab","size":B_tab}`.
Its data line is `__bennett_rt_qtab SP HEX LF`, with HEX exactly `B_tab` zero bytes, encoded
as `2·B_tab` lowercase `0` digits. Producers MAY stream this complete image, but MUST emit
every byte; ISA 0.9 has no implicit zero-fill/BSS or sparse data encoding. The data-symbol
size, decoded image length and layout-global size MUST be equal (ISA §9.1–§9.2). E4/ISA
§16.1 permits empty HEX only for a zero-byte data line and cannot encode this positive-size
table. Compute interface/closure/object hashes from the complete canonical image and final
layout under ISA §9.2; no hashes of an empty placeholder may survive. The image initialises
the issued-tag count and every state to 0 (§6.5), so the first fresh tag is 1 even if its ISA
id is 0. This initialisation occurs at program load, not at each device entry; subsequent
entries retain the table and R reversal restores its earlier bytes.

**Required layout/image vectors (LQ-1–LQ-5).** These are normative conformance inputs and
expected layouts for DD, DM and DB (§21.3); the vectors do not claim execution of a
compiler-generated table. Use pw=64, `null_size`=4096, target alignment 8 for the ordinary
eight-byte `@saved` at [4096,4104), `heap_limit_max`=0 and frozen stack capacity 32.
`@saved` retains a clean qalloc handle across entry return, so §6.5 requires the table.
Unless stated otherwise set `B_tab`=4096; then `C_tab`=8, `Q_tab`=255, records occupy
4080 bytes and the remaining 8 tail bytes are zero.

| Vector | Ordinary data and coherent regions | Resolved private table | H / A_tab / S_cells |
|---|---|---|---|
| LQ-1: saved handle + `MEM_IN(size=1)` | data_end=4104, heap_base=heap_end=8192; no arena; `m0:region[0,8] in` at [8192,8193); regions_end=8193 | qtab_base=12288, range [12288,16384) | 14 / 2 / 5 |
| LQ-2: saved handle + arena | shadow [4104,8200), data_end=8200, heap_base=heap_end=12288; one may-Co byte at arena offset 0 and coherent-heap capacity 4095 give arena/`coh:region[12,8] in` [12288,16384); no other regions; regions_end=16384 | qtab_base=16384, range [16384,20480) | 15 / 2 / 5 |
| LQ-3: all shapes counted | ordinary data/heap as LQ-1; one oracle has LQ-1's `m0`; another has `m0:region[13,8] in` at [8192,16384); regions_end=16384 regardless of emission order | qtab_base=16384, range [16384,20480) | 15 / 2 / 5 |

In LQ-1 there are two classical globals (`saved`, table) and one interface pre-entry, so
P=3 and A_tab=2. LQ-2 has three globals (`saved`, shadow, table) plus `coh`, so P=4 and
A_tab=2; the relocated may-Co byte is not another classical global. LQ-3 has P=3 because
the two oracles' interface entries are counted by their maximum, not their sum. Native
text_base remains 4194304 in all three. The former heap-end placement collided with `m0`
at [8192,8193) in LQ-1 and with `coh` at [12288,16384) in LQ-2; the new ranges admit both.
The LQ-3 maximum prevents a later shape from colliding with the table.

LQ-1 also MUST retain the unchanged D29 default `B_tab`=2^40=1099511627776: its table is
[12288,1099511640064), H=41, `Q_tab`=68719476735 and HEX length=2199023255552.
All other addresses, heap/stack capacities and A_tab remain as above. Computing this
layout and length needs no materialised terabyte fixture; it does not waive the complete
image requirement for an emitted object. At pw=32 the unchanged default is 2^29 bytes;
the same low ranges and table base give qtab_end=536883200 and H=30, within 2^32.

LQ-4 isolates the image requirement using LQ-1's disjoint 4096-byte table. Emit a data
signature and global of size 4096, and exactly 8192 zero hex digits after the mandatory
separator. ISA `hash` followed by `check` with the computed interface hashes MUST admit
the complete object; initial loads of the count, first record id/state and last table byte
MUST all yield zero. Changing only HEX to empty while retaining both declared sizes MUST
fail admission for a data-size mismatch. The former empty-image requirement made even
this disjoint object inadmissible. Full object checks MUST also reject LQ-1/LQ-2 with the
table moved back to the old heap-end base, holding their complete images fixed; omitting
the table from a restricted reference object is not a substitute for these checks.

LQ-5 checks that placement does not reject a fitting table near the address-space limit.
Use pw=32 and LQ-1's ordinary data and empty heap. One oracle's four `in` regions, in order,
have A=30,30,29,28 and W=8, hence ranges [1073741824,2147483648),
[2147483648,3221225472), [3221225472,3758096384), [3758096384,4026531840).
The unchanged default table size 2^29 fits at [8192,536879104) in the first alignment gap;
H=32, A_tab=3 (two globals plus four regions), S_cells=5, and native text_base=4026531840.
Appending that table at regions_end would end at 4563402752, outside 2^32, despite this
available gap. That must not cause a layout error or a smaller default. Check the default
arithmetically, and admit a full-image B_tab=4096 control with table [8192,12288) and the
same interface addresses, H/A_tab/S_cells and native text base.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 19.9 Options, identity, outputs, module flag

| Option | Meaning | Default |
|---|---|---|
| `-bennett-dialect=<v>` | MUST equal the plugin's dialect version (`BD-E-VERSION`) | required |
| `-bennett-isa=<v>` | MUST equal the ISA version the plugin targets (`BD-E-VERSION`) | `0.8` |
| `--bennett-budgets=<file>`, `--bennett-budget-default=<s,h,st,d,ss>` | §9.2 | §9.2 defaults |
| `--bennett-class=<name>` | §3.4 | none |
| `--bennett-tier=reversible`, `--bennett-entry=<sym>`, `--bennett-disk=<file>` | §19.2 | – |
| `--bennett-oracle=<sym>`, `--bennett-bind=…`, `--bennett-dev=…`, `--bennett-payload=…` | §19.3 | – |
| `--bennett-snapshot=<dump>` | §19.6 | – |
| `-bennett-closed-unit` | §19.7 | off |
| `--bennett-platform=<profile>` | §20 | none |
| `--bennett-vscale=<V>`, `--bennett-cpu=<model>` | §12.5, §15.2 | §24 |
| `--bennett-qreg-instances=<n>` | §6.5 | 64 |
| `-bennett-inplace-exhaustive-bits=<n>` | §8.7 | 24 |
| `-bennett-warn-qstore` | §6.7 | off |
| `-bennett-out=<dir>` | the outputs below | required |

**Identity.** `llvmGetPassPluginInfo` returns plugin name `bennett` and version string
`dialect-0.2.12;isa-0.8`; the driver refuses a plugin whose string differs. [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23] One plugin build per LLVM
major (NS §3.2).

**Outputs** (in `-bennett-out`, file names plugin-defined, §24 D22): the materialised device
variants as bitcode (§17.7); the host module with device entries replaced by launch stubs; the ISA
objects (ISA §9, §16); the certificate (ISA §12) with `producer.materialisation = {llvm_version,
pipeline, normaliser_version: "0.2.8"}` and `bindings.dialect_mapping_version = "0.2.9"`; the coverage
report (ISA §13), whose `source` field is `dialect-0.2.12:<rule id>` (e.g. `dialect-0.2.12:N33`); the
blocker reports (§25.1); and the **build record**, one JCS object with the build flags, the
budget table (resolved, including each shape's selection_identity, final semantic identity,
resolved components and frozen layout capacities under §9.2) and its SHA-256, the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes, the snapshot
hash, the layout options and the `cpuid` leaf table (§15.2). Its SHA-256 is recorded as the build metadata of the certificate's
`producer.version` (`<driver version>+build.<hex>`) until the ISA certificate has a field for it
(§25.2). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2]

**A missing plugin fails loud.** No production artefact defines a marker: `bennett-runtimes`,
`bennett-arith` and the driver's link inputs define no `__bennett_*` marker symbol (test runtimes
such as the trace runtime do, and are never linked into production). A link in which the plugin
did not run therefore leaves undefined references and fails at link time; the driver MUST NOT
ignore unresolved symbols or supply weak marker definitions.

**Module flag.** A producer MAY add `!{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.2.12"}` (behaviour Error, so an
LTO link of modules of two dialect versions fails); the plugin MUST reject a module whose flag
differs from its version (`BD-E-VERSION`). [deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-23]

```llvm example=version-flag verdict=reject:BD-E-VERSION
define void @top() {
  %h = call ptr @__bennett_qalloc(i64 0, i64 1)
  call void @__bennett_qfree(ptr %h)
  ret void
}

!llvm.module.flags = !{!0}
!0 = !{i32 1, !"bennett.dialect", !"0.1"}
```

[deps: DLT-19.9, DLT-25.2, DLT-18.7, ISA-12.1-P7, ISA-12.2-P1, ISA-12.3-P2, ISA-12.3-P3] Labels are the checker's (ISA K74): a producer label is a checked request.

## 23. Versioning

- The **full version string** of this document (`0.2.12`) is the unit of compatibility: a plugin
  implements exactly one dialect version and targets exactly one ISA version (§19.9); drafts are
  not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-23, DLT-19.9]
- The consumer-gap dispositions revise the full dialect to `0.2.12`. [deps: DLT-5.2, DLT-12.2-P11, DLT-23]
  The lifting map remains `0.2.9` (global-numbering correction); `norm`, `handles` and
  `regions` remain `0.2.8`; unchanged oracle-abi, tdl and effects remain `0.2.4`, marker/header,
  asm and corpus remain `0.2`, platform and driver remain `0.2.1`. Every reference object is checked
  again; unchanged ISA-0.8 interface signatures retain their hashes. The map version
  is recorded in certificate metadata, not inserted into ISA-owned interface signatures.
- Every semantic change bumps the version; the reference objects and hashes of §16 depend on the
  ISA version string, the map and the layout, and change with any of them (0.1's hashes changed
  with the wrapper form of §16.2).
- The build record (§19.9) records the runtimes, arith and asm-table hashes and the budget-table
  hash; `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml` records `dialect` and the targeted `isa`; the change rules are in
  `spec/README.md`.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
## 23. Versioning

- The **full version string** of this document (`0.3`) is the unit of compatibility: a plugin
  implements exactly one dialect version and targets exactly one ISA version (§19.9); drafts are
  not compatible with each other. [deps: DLT-23, DLT-19.9]
- The move to ISA 0.9 revises the full dialect to `0.3`, with handles, regions, oracle-abi, tdl, effects, map, platform and
  driver at `0.3`. `norm` remains `0.2.8`; marker/header, asm and corpus remain `0.2`. Every reference object and hash is
  recomputed (ISA §9.2). 0.2.x objects remain valid 0.2.x artefacts of ISA 0.8. The map version

# spec/

`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md` is draft **0.2.12** (2026-11-01), from eight consumer-named gaps,
awaiting council review. It supersedes accepted draft 0.2.11 (round 10, sol/codex);
accepted 0.2.10 is retained in git, with binding interpretations in
[`errata-0.2.10.md`](errata-0.2.10.md). Binding interpretations of 0.2.12 itself (text and SHA
unchanged) are in [`errata-0.2.12.md`](errata-0.2.12.md). The reviewer disposition table is in
[`../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md`](../docs/briefs/2026-11-01-dialect-0212-consumer-gaps.md).
It targets
ISA 0.8 (`../bennett-isa-spec/spec/`, settled) and is graded against it. `bennett_markers.h` is the
normative C/C++ header of the marker ABI (section 3.3). `Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.2.PROGRESS.md` is
the writing job's log. Council records and the runner live in the `bennett` meta repo
(`docs/council/`, `tools/council.py`); the rubric is `docs/council/rubric-isa.md`, under which any
narrowing of north-star scope is S0.

Rules for changing it:

- **No narrowing** (CLAUDE.md rule 10). A compile-time rejection needs a row in section 22.1 citing
  the north-star clause or maintainer decision that requires it. A deferral (section 24) postpones
  mechanics only and states the capability it keeps. A capability ISA 0.8 cannot express is a
  blocker (section 25.1) with the exact ISA change and a `blocked:` verdict, never a rejection.
- Every semantic change bumps the full version string (header and section 23); drafts are not
  compatible with each other. The reference objects and hashes of section 16 depend on the ISA
  version string, the lifting map and the layout, so they change with any of them.
- **Checked examples** (section 1.5, process rules P-1 to P-3):
  - P-1: every rule id has an `accept` example, every rejection a `reject` example, every blocker
    a `blocked` example; Appendix B is the index, and a rule without one fails the check.
  - P-2: an example the checker cannot yet execute fails (`checker-unimplemented: <capability>`);
    no example is removed or weakened to make the checker pass; the freeze gate is all green.
  - P-3: executed results come from independent executors (LLVM tools, native binaries, the Rust
    ISA reference interpreter `bennett-isa-ref`); Python computes expected artefacts only.
- Run `python3 scripts/dialect_check_v02.py` before anyone grades this draft; it must exit 0. (For 0.2 the
  checker is being extended against the Rust interpreter in a separate job; until then the 0.2
  examples were executed by the writing job as its progress file records.)
- Add every new example or stated result as a tagged block, so the checker executes it; an
  untagged `llvm` or `isa` block is an error. A `semantics=` example must be byte-equal to its
  `corpus/legality/cases/spec-*.ll` file; run `python3 corpus/legality/harness/run.py baseline
  --majors 18,20,23 --cases 'cases/spec-*'` (headless, CLAUDE.md rule 11) after changing one.
- Write derivations out before stating numbers; every stated example result must be computed.
- Surgical edits only; a rewrite has introduced a mechanical error every time it was tried (ISA
  rounds 1-8).
- Revisions go back through the council.
# Binding interpretations of accepted dialect 0.2.10

Published with draft 0.2.12, 2026-11-01. Accepted text at `023f5a771a814270634db3be9cfde88ded69f975`, spec SHA-256 `d4f3c1a88391cc147f8c8fa4f80f82852e3ebe6f880610fc16cd5151f33d7206`, is unchanged; consumer pins remain valid. These interpretations resolve ambiguity in that text; new 0.2.12 ABIs/accuracy requirements are identified separately and are not retroactive ABI changes.

## mkb — §§5.2, 7.2, 12.2 N0

**N0 and resolution order.** Before testing the forwarding-thunk predicate or freezing candidates, the plugin MUST apply the N0 passes of §12.2 to the virtual device bodies used by S2; §7.2 resolution then uses those bodies, and §7.3 hashes their materialised closures.

The plugin MUST retain A(S) for every original source function or alias whose address reaches the region operand, dispatching that address to its post-N0 resolved candidate even when N0 removes the forwarding call; a declaration remaining after N0 is still F(8), but an intrinsic call removed by N0 MUST NOT turn its containing defined body into an external candidate.

## jjr — §§16.9, 17.1

The thin malloc body in §16.9 is a specialised witness whose calls all request 16 bytes. ISA allocation itself is exactly byte-bumping, not aligned; C malloc still owes its target ABI alignment. The general wrapper rounds its requested extent and inserts explicit charged padding allocations when an intervening runtime primitive leaves the next address unaligned, identically in both builds. This resolves the example-versus-C-ABI ambiguity without altering ISA §7.1.

## lbq — §§11.2–11.3, 14.7, 19.2

**Console syscall framing.** For virtual stdout/stderr, runtimes MUST implement `writev` by validating the vector count and total length before the first effect, then emitting one `console.write` per nonempty vector in source order (zero-length writes emit no record), returning the total byte count; the descriptor ABI limits are 0..1024 vectors and total at most target SSIZE_MAX, with invalid counts/overflow returning EINVAL and a null nonempty vector table returning EFAULT.

The console shim MUST return ENOTTY for `ioctl(TIOCGWINSZ)`, ESPIPE for seeking, and success for closing a virtual stdout/stderr descriptor, with no extra device effect or terminal metadata; this close releases no host resource and does not disable the virtual byte sink.

**Model history.** R console writes MUST use only the ISA effect tape, while L model state is ordinary reversible memory initialised from descriptor bindings, private and discarding only under the oracle rules of §11.3; action console capacity exhaustion MUST fault before mutation under TDL-12, and a standalone native-model test undo log MUST NOT substitute for either tier's history. No second production model-history ABI is required.

**Process context.** Before entering `<sym>`, the driver/runtimes preparation MUST establish a static single-thread musl process context with the supplied argc/argv/envp bytes, a null-terminated argv and envp, `environ`, program name, initial fenv and the libc/stdio state needed by the linked program; comparison-only Linux `_start` is permitted, but Linux ELF crt/TLS/auxv entry is not required in device tiers.

For a C `main` entry, the runtimes entry wrapper MUST treat normal return as `exit(status)`, running registered exit handlers and flushing stdio exactly once under §14.7 before the launch stub catches the exit pseudo-exception; explicit `exit` follows that same path, and `_Exit` bypasses handlers/flush as §14.7 specifies.

## 0na — §§3.1, 17.7, 19.5

The “nobody: primitives” ownership row applies to production bitcode before ISA lowering, not native comparison support implementing those primitives after materialisation. Such support must preserve device layout and history. Draft 0.2.12 §19.5 publishes the new v1 layout transport ABI; that spelling is not imposed retroactively on 0.2.10. The existing memory/fault outcomes and North Star definedness classifiers are not waived by UBSan-only support.

## dfd — §§13.1, 13.5

The explicit exact-conversion and correct-rounding guarantees take priority over APFloat implementation fallbacks. PPCDoubleDouble denotes high+low, not the legacy consecutive-106-bit approximation. A low component may not be discarded when the exact sum is representable in the destination. Sparse-tail loss and intermediate unsigned-low saturation are not permitted by the named-format reference. Draft 0.2.12 §13.1 makes canonical-pair rounding and flags explicit; no claim that the current arith publication already meets these guarantees is made.

## 1av — §§13.4–13.5

“No correct-rounding claim” does not authorise an arbitrary deterministic non-numerical substitute for the named operation or waive the specified IEEE flags. The accepted text does not quantify its numerical error. The one-local-ulp carrier bound and precise constrained accuracy/flag policy in draft 0.2.12 are NEW requirements, not retroactive assertions that 0.2.10 contained a numeric bound or that measured sweeps prove a full-domain bound.

## z7u — §13.1 arbitrary-format row

“Format named by metadata” means the LLVM major’s named interpretation MDString and its format/carrier semantics, not an unbounded user-created APFloat precision/exponent schema. LLVM 23 Intrinsics.td and LangRef define to(value, format, rounding, saturation) and from(bits, format). Draft 0.2.12 publishes NEW concrete carrier operand spellings and a static request/manifest protocol; those new ABI details are not claimed to have existed in 0.2.10.

## 21a — §§13.1, 13.5

The generic “soft-float call of §13.1 with suffix _rm” does not merge the distinct LLVM quiet fcmp and signaling fcmps operations. Quiet-NaN invalid behavior must remain distinguishable. Draft 0.2.12 publishes the NEW cmps_B_rm spelling and exact operand/exception lowering in §13.5; the absence of an earlier spelling was a contract gap, not authority to silently substitute quiet comparison.

/* Independent primitive/wrapper trace fixture, not a production allocator. */
#include <stdint.h>
#include <stdio.h>
#include <string.h>
#include <stddef.h>
#include <limits.h>
struct record { uint64_t base, requested, old_bump; };
struct heap { unsigned char bytes[32]; uint64_t bump,count,limit; struct record records[32]; };
static unsigned alloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t extent=n?n:1;
 if(extent>h->limit || h->bump>h->limit-extent || h->count==32)return 2;
 *p=4096+h->bump;
 h->records[h->count++]=(struct record){*p,n,h->bump};
 memset(h->bytes+h->bump,0,extent);h->bump+=extent;return 0;
}
static unsigned cmalloc(struct heap *h,uint64_t n,uint64_t *p){
 uint64_t size=n?n:1,unused;
 if(size>UINT64_MAX-15)return 2;
 uint64_t extent=(size+15)&~UINT64_C(15),pad=(-(4096+h->bump))&15;
 if(pad){unsigned code=alloc(h,pad,&unused);if(code)return code;}
 return alloc(h,extent,p);
}
/* Reverse the ordinary primitive allocation, restoring its pre-state bytes. */
static void reverse(struct heap *h){
 struct record r=h->records[--h->count];
 memset(h->bytes+r.old_bump,0xaa,h->bump-r.old_bump);h->bump=r.old_bump;
 memset(h->records+h->count,0,sizeof r);
}
int main(void){
 struct heap h={0};memset(h.bytes,0xaa,sizeof h.bytes);h.limit=32;
 struct heap initial=h;uint64_t p,q;unsigned a=alloc(&h,3,&p),b=cmalloc(&h,1,&q);
 printf("[%u,%u,%llu,%llu,%llu,%llu]\n",a,b,(unsigned long long)p,(unsigned long long)q,(unsigned long long)(4096+h.bump),(unsigned long long)h.count);
 for(unsigned k=0;k<h.count;k++)printf("[%llu,%llu]\n",(unsigned long long)h.records[k].base,(unsigned long long)h.records[k].requested);
 unsigned nonzero=0;for(unsigned k=0;k<32;k++)nonzero|=h.bytes[k];printf("%u\n",nonzero);

 code=cmalloc(&h,UINT64_MAX,&q);printf("[%u,%d]\n",code,!memcmp(&saved,&h,sizeof h));

}
/* Dialect §§3.1,16.9,17.1: allocation is an ISA primitive, not
 * a private C arena. Its live table and nonreclaiming history belong to ISA.
 * The native comparison link supplies the same primitive semantics separately.
 */
#include <stddef.h>
#include <stdint.h>

extern void *__bennett_nz_alloc(uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_nz_free(void *);
extern void __bennett_nz_fault(uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_rt_memset(void *, unsigned char, uint64_t);
extern void __bennett_rt_memmove(void *, const void *, uint64_t);

/* Bookkeeping is ordinary, charged device memory, not an ISA-private query
 * or a capped native map. Pool slots stay live and are reused as data after
 * their C payload is freed. Payload storage/history are never reclaimed. */
struct c_allocation { void *base; uint64_t requested, live; struct c_allocation *next; };
_Static_assert(sizeof(struct c_allocation)==32,"C allocation record layout");
static struct c_allocation *live_allocations, *spare_records;

/* Tracks the result of an ordinary primitive request. This is a local cursor,
 * never an authoritative view of direct primitive users' allocation history.
 * It is ordinary device memory and reverses with the caller. */
static uintptr_t next_byte;
void *__bennett_rt_alloc(uint64_t n) {
    void *p = __bennett_nz_alloc(n);
    next_byte = (uintptr_t)p + (n ? n : 1);
    return p;
}

void *__bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(size_t a,size_t n) {
    if (!a || (a&(a-1))) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(4);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    if (a<_Alignof(max_align_t)) a=_Alignof(max_align_t);
    /* Requested alignment controls the starting address. The allocation's
     * byte extent retains the C ABI's max_align_t rounding. */
    const size_t unit = _Alignof(max_align_t);
    size_t extent = n ? n : 1;
    if (extent > SIZE_MAX - (unit - 1)) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(2);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    extent = (extent + unit - 1) / unit * unit;
    /* Direct primitive users can have advanced the heap since our last call,
     * and §19.5 only guarantees page alignment of the initial base. Refresh
     * through an ordinary charged zero allocation (physical extent one), then
     * request the minimal padding from that exact next byte. No private ISA
     * cursor/query or native-only state is used. */
    __bennett_rt_alloc(0);
    size_t padding = (-(uintptr_t)next_byte) % a;
    if (padding) __bennett_rt_alloc(padding);
    void *p = __bennett_rt_alloc(extent);
    /* The primitive guarantees zero fill; no redundant byte loop is needed. */
    struct c_allocation *r=spare_records;
    if (r) spare_records=r->next;
    else r=__bennett_rt_alloc(sizeof *r);
    r->base=p; r->requested=n; r->live=1; r->next=live_allocations;
    live_allocations=r;
    return p;
}

void *malloc(size_t n) { return __bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(_Alignof(max_align_t),n); }

void *calloc(size_t count, size_t size) {
    if (size && count > SIZE_MAX / size) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(2); /* FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP; no mutation on overflow */
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    return malloc(count * size);
}

void free(void *p) {
    if (!p) return;
    __bennett_nz_free(p); /* reject interior/dead/nonheap before C state changes */
    struct c_allocation **link=&live_allocations;
    while (*link) {
        struct c_allocation *r=*link;
        if (r->base==p) {
            *link=r->next; r->live=0; r->next=spare_records; spare_records=r;
            return;
        }
        link=&r->next;
    }
    /* Runtime raw allocations can be freed without a C allocation record. */
}

void *__bennett_rt_aligned_realloc(void *p,size_t n,size_t a) {
    if (!p) return __bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(a,n);
    struct c_allocation *r=live_allocations;
    while (r && r->base!=p) r=r->next;
    if (!r || !r->live) {
        __bennett_nz_fault(7);
        __builtin_unreachable();
    }
    uint64_t count=r->requested;
    if (count>n) count=n;
    /* Always move; zero uses malloc(0)'s unique allocation policy. Failed
     * growth has not freed the old payload. */
    void *q=__bennett_rt_aligned_alloc(a,n);
    __bennett_rt_memmove(q,p,count);
    free(p);
    return q;
}

void *realloc(void *p,size_t n) {
    return __bennett_rt_aligned_realloc(p,n,_Alignof(max_align_t));
}

 succeeded in 76ms:
### 7.1 Classical memory (CC, R)

Byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse. `layout` partitions the address
space: a **null region** `[0, null_size)` never allocated (null_size ≥
1); globals at fixed bases with images; the heap `[heap_base, heap_base +
heap_limit_max)`, all disjoint, `heap_base + heap_limit_max ≤ 2^ptr_
width`, and the number of globals ≤ 2^A_tab, all validated at load. `p =
alloc n` **fails before any mutation** when bump + max(n, 1) > heap_base
+ heap_limit_max or the table holds 2^A_tab entries (CC trap / R fault
`FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`); otherwise it returns the bump pointer, records
`(base, size = n, live = 1, pinned = 0)` and advances by `max(n, 1)`;
globals have pre-entered pinned live entries; an access is valid iff its
whole byte range lies in one live entry; `free p` marks the unique live
unpinned entry with base p dead (unpinned entries are heap entries,
whose bases strictly increase; pinned entries cannot be freed and a
pinned entry with base p is left unchanged; no live unpinned entry with
base p: CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT`); storage is never
reclaimed.
Initial state: bump = `heap_base`, table = the pre-entries in layout
order, memory zero except images.


### 7.4 Run history and the dynamic SSA model (CC, R)

The classical environment holds **dynamic instances**: every executed
defining instruction creates a new instance retained in the run history
until reversal; a name denotes its most recent instance. Frames:

| Instruction | Frame |
|---|---|
| any defining instruction | its defined instance |
| `br` | predecessor block, prior block-parameter instances |
| `store` | address, width, overwritten bytes |
| `alloc` | old bump pointer, the table entry written, the zero-filled range |
| `free` | the entry's old live bit |
| `call` | invocation boundary (callee, argument values) |
| `ret` | result values, the callee's activation and the frame link |
| `uncall` | the reversed segment's id (skipped by whole-run reversal, below) |
| `effect` | tape index plus the device-specific record of §7.3; reversal also removes its effect-tape entry |
| `hostcall` | payload bytes and reply bytes |
| `qreg.*`, `measure` | the ledger record of §3.3 (ids, class, widths, prior states, whether a free's cleanliness was verified) and the measured bits |
| `budget.mint` | the token and its tuple; reversal revokes the token |
| `oracle_call` / `action_call` | (region hash, variant, inherited control count, classical args with every token's tuple, register ids): reversed by re-application / adjoint, exactly once, by this frame |
| fault record (a failing instruction, `trap`, `fault`) | cause code (§4.9) and program point; terminal. Reversal (§10) removes the record; the failing instruction had no other effect, so the state is then the state before it |

**Logical history.** Frames and dynamic instances specify the
*logical* history. An executor MAY store it by checkpoints, deltas or
deterministic replay, provided it reconstructs the same logical frames,
instances, reversal marks and traversal order; it never re-issues an
external event (effects are replayed from the effect tape and host
replies from their frames, never requested again), never re-executes a
region call to recover its record, and never copies quantum state. T4 is
stated over the logical history. **Active and audit history.** The
*active* history is the sequence of frames not marked reversed;
reversed frames and `uncall` markers remain as immutable *audit
records*. Undoing a defining frame removes its dynamic instance from the
active environment although its audit record is retained.

**Pure frames.** A frame is **pure** iff it belongs to `br` or to a
defining instruction among `const`, the binops, `udiv..srem`, `icmp`,
`select`, `zext sext trunc`, `ptrtoint inttoptr`, `load`, `qreg.width
qreg.id qreg.from_id`: it changes only the set of instances. Every other
frame (`store alloc free call ret uncall trap fault effect hostcall
qreg.new qreg.free qreg.slice qreg.concat measure budget.mint`, region
calls, fault records) is impure.

**History traversal** (the one reversal procedure, used by segment
`uncall` and by whole-run reversal). A range of frames is **reversed**
by visiting its frames in LIFO order; a frame already marked reversed
and an `uncall` frame are **skipped**; every other frame is undone by
its row of the table above and then marked reversed. Reversed frames
stay in the history, marked, and are never undone again. An `uncall`
marker is always skipped: when a later traversal (an enclosing
segment's `uncall`, or whole-run reversal) reaches it, the frames of the
segment it names are already marked and are skipped too, so neither the
marker nor its segment is undone, re-executed or redone a second time.

**Segment uncall** (`uncall @f (args) (results)`). Let S be the most
recent completed call segment of the history (a `call` frame, its
matching `ret` frame and every frame between them). The `uncall` is
legal iff S is a call of `@f`, S's recorded boundary argument and result
values equal the operand values, **every frame after S is pure**, and S
contains, at any nesting depth, **no `measure` frame** and no `qreg.
free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (hardware, §17 item 14);
otherwise it is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP` and changes nothing
beyond the fault record (§4.9). Execution reverses S by the history traversal (nested segments are
undone; nested segments already cancelled by an inner `uncall`, and that
inner `uncall` frame, are skipped), and pushes an `uncall` frame naming
S. The instances defined inside S no longer exist; using such a name
before it is redefined is a CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_TRAP`.
**Whole-run reversal** (§10) is the history traversal over the whole
history: S's effects on memory, the allocation table, devices, the
register ledger and tokens were undone by the `uncall`, and the frames between S and the
`uncall` are pure, so at the `uncall` frame every component except the
retained instances already equals its value before S. T4 is stated over
these frames. *(non-normative)* S is never re-executed and never redone;
the compute–copy–`uncall` idiom derives what it keeps through pure
instructions before the `uncall`. Suite A contains: `f` calls `g`,
uncalls `g`, returns; `g` performs one `clock.read`; `call f; uncall f`
leaves the clock at its entry value; and `uncall` of a segment that
measured is a trap.


## 10. Launch ABI (launch 0.9)

**Model.** A run executes a device CC entry; the host passes classical
arguments, register ids and the entropy tape, receives classical
results, fault codes and declared measurements; host services are
reached by `hostcall`. **Positional binding**: argument i of `bennett_
run` binds to parameter i of the entry's signature: `INT(width, bytes)`
to `iN` with width = N (else `E_ARG`), `F64` to `f64`, `QREG` to `qreg[n]`
with the register's n or to `qreg[*]` with any width, `TOKEN` to `token`
(a valid token, §5.4). Arguments and the entropy tape
are **copied** at `bennett_run`; the caller may free its buffers
afterwards. Host-minted tokens are scoped to the program and destroyed
at unload; device-minted tokens to their run (§5.4).
Accessor-returned pointers are valid until the owning object is freed.
`bennett_unload` with a run not CLOSED: `E_INVALID_STATE`.

**Invocation tree.** A run record is a tree (CC segments, R calls, Q
calls); an R call's subtree is owned by its R history; `bennett_reverse`
reverses post-order: CC segments by their run history, R calls by LIFO
reversal of their history (§7.4, reversed segments and `uncall` frames
skipped), top-level Q calls by re-application / adjoint. A fault record
is terminal (§4.9): the run is `FAILED`, and every activation open at
the fault keeps its frames, which reversal undoes like those of a call
that returned.

**States.** `CREATED → RUNNING → (SUSPENDED ⇄ RUNNING) → (HOST_REQUEST ⇄
RUNNING) → COMPLETED | FAILED`; `COMPLETED → REVERSED`; `FAILED →
REVERSED`; any of
`COMPLETED/FAILED/REVERSED → CLOSED` (`bennett_run_close` releases the
record; register ids created by the run remain valid unless measured or
freed). A run owns the registers and memory it touches exclusively while
not CLOSED; a register reached by `qreg.from_id` is touched from then
on; `bennett_qreg_prepare`, `bennett_qreg_free` and a `bennett_run`
argument naming a register owned by another run that is not CLOSED are
`E_INVALID_STATE`. **Reverse eligibility**: `COMPLETED` or `FAILED`; no `measure`
frame; no `qreg.free` frame whose cleanliness was not verified (§17
item 14); no register it touched has been prepared, freed, sliced or
used by another run since; else `E_NOT_REVERSIBLE`. Reversing a `FAILED`
run first undoes its terminal fault record, then traverses the rest of
its history exactly as for a completed run, including the frames of
activations that never returned; the run is then `REVERSED` with every
component T4 names at its entry value. `bennett_qreg_prepare` and `bennett_
qreg_free` require a clean register (`E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN` otherwise on a
verifying executor; a non-verifying one: §17 item 14); there
is no reset and no trace-out: a register in unknown state is released by
a `measure` inside a run, which consumes its handle (§3.3).

**Results.** `bennett_result_get` writes value i and sets `*kind` and
`*width`: `INT` as ⌈N/8⌉ little-endian bytes, `*width` = N; `F64` as the
8 IEEE 754 binary64 little-endian bytes, `*width` = 64; `QREG` and
`TOKEN` as the 8-byte little-endian id, `*width` = 64. A `cap` smaller
than that byte count is `E_ARG` and nothing is written. Fault records
are read by `bennett_result_nfaults` / `bennett_result_fault` (codes of
§4.9; a `COMPLETED` run has none, a `FAILED` run has exactly one, its
terminal record, and exposes no values).
For an R `fault C`, `bennett_wait` returns `OK` with `*result_out` set
and `*request_out` NULL as for any `FAILED` run;
`bennett_result_nvalues` is 0, `bennett_result_nfaults` is 1 and
`bennett_result_fault(result, 0)` is exactly C when C ≠ 0 (4 when C = 0,
§5.4), with all 64 bits preserved. The fault code is result data, not a
`bennett_status` error or a host-service reply.

**Waiting.** `bennett_wait` blocks until the run leaves `RUNNING` and
returns `OK` with exactly one outcome: `HOST_REQUEST` (`*request_out`
set, `*result_out` NULL; `bennett_reply` answers it and the run is
`RUNNING` again) or `COMPLETED` / `FAILED` (`*result_out` set, `*request_
out` NULL). Waiting on a `SUSPENDED`, `REVERSED` or `CLOSED` run is `E_
INVALID_STATE`.

**`hostcall`.** `rlen = hostcall service ptr len`: `[ptr, ptr + len)` MUST
lie in one live allocation (else CC trap and no request is made); those
bytes are copied into the request; the run enters `HOST_REQUEST`; the
reply payload is written to `[ptr, ptr + min(len, reply length))` and
`rlen` = the full reply length (so truncation is detectable; a reply
whose length does not fit `ptr_width` bits is refused by `bennett_reply`
with `E_ARG` before any write, and the run stays in `HOST_REQUEST`); the frame
records the request bytes, the overwritten bytes and the reply bytes.
Every `service` is declared in `devices.services`; an undeclared one is
a load error.

**Tape.** `bennett_tape_next` yields each `effect` in tape order; `args`
holds the operation's operands in the order of §7.3, each an unsigned
64-bit little-endian field (pointers zero-extended from `ptr_width`), so
`*alen` = 8 × arity.

```c
/* launch 0.9. All objects opaque. A function that returns an object transfers ownership to the caller,
   released with the matching *_free. Status 0 = BENNETT_OK. */
typedef enum { BENNETT_OK=0, BENNETT_E_VERSION, BENNETT_E_PTR_WIDTH, BENNETT_E_DEVICE_MISSING, BENNETT_E_GATE_UNSUPPORTED,
  BENNETT_E_TARGET, BENNETT_E_BUDGET, BENNETT_E_QREG_NOT_CLEAN, BENNETT_E_INVALID_STATE, BENNETT_E_NOT_REVERSIBLE,

bennett_status bennett_suspend(bennett_program_t*, bennett_run_id);
```

### 10.1 Launch-estimate contract (K12)

**LAUNCH-ESTIMATE** (K12; b-isa-spec-c15, from bennett-vm-hsd).
The 0.9 request carries the complete resolved parameter vector.

**Resolved request.** `bennett_estimate_run` MUST copy and
validate `params_jcs`, the JCS array of **all** classical arguments in
signature order, encoded as §12.3 specifies; `params_len` is its UTF-8
byte length, excluding any NUL terminator. The separately supplied
valid token of that program fixes `[B,heap,stack,depth]`; token-typed
arguments fix their own tuples, never the outer tuple by default.
Host- and live device-minted tokens are both admitted (§5.4); the
request copies the tuple while valid, so later revocation cannot
retarget an already returned estimate.
An empty array binds an empty signature only; no argument is implicitly
zero, inferred from a hash, or taken from an earlier run. A missing,
extra, mistyped or out-of-range argument is `E_ARG` before producing an
estimate. Every well-typed resolved argument vector is supported; the
interface bundles, arbitrary target/fault entries, dirty wires and
interface cells remain quantum inputs, not chosen basis values.

The caller supplies a nonempty `variant` and `inherited_controls` as
in §11; these are part of the request, not inferred from a different
invocation. The request denotes that region/variant/control count at
its declared accuracy and those resolved parameters and budget.
It uses the object's schedule exactly when every §5.4/§11 binding
matches, otherwise the §11 defaults. The estimator MUST resolve the
complete generated realisation and aggregate its scheduled leaf events
and wire live intervals by §8.2; running one basis input, counting only
executed oracle rows instead of all padded B steps, or silently changing
parameters, budget, target, expansion library or cleanup is not an
estimate of that request. A consumer lacking the selected expansion
library reports `E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE` as §11 requires (b-isa-spec-8r4);
this postpones mechanics, not the estimate capability.

**Resource rows.** `estimate_nresources` counts scalar rows,
not target resource declarations. A `count` or `time` resource has one
row named by its escaped resource name. Each `occupancy` or `peak`
resource has one row **for every target class**, including unused classes with value
zero, named `escape(resource)[escape(class)]`; no sum across classes is
substituted. `escape` percent-encodes the UTF-8 bytes except ASCII
letters, digits, `-._~`, using uppercase hex (including `%`, `[` and
`]`). Scalar resource names use the same escape. Rows are enumerated
by increasing bytewise encoded name. Thus names are injective even when
target names contain delimiters; `name` and `value_decimal` remain
ordinary NUL-terminated strings with §10's ownership lifetime.

**Decimal values.** Aggregation is exact rational arithmetic;
rounding occurs only at the accessor boundary. `value_decimal` is the
least nonnegative decimal with at most **34 significant digits** that
is ≥ the exact resource value. For q > 0 let e be the unique integer
with 10^e ≤ q < 10^(e+1); return
`ceil(q / 10^(e−33)) × 10^(e−33)`. Zero is `0`. The canonical spelling
uses ASCII digits and an optional decimal point, no exponent, plus
sign, leading integer zeros (except `0`), trailing fractional zeros or
negative zero. Exact integers and terminating rationals that fit are
unchanged. This is an upper enclosure, not a fabricated exact value for
1/3; capacity, matching, objective comparison and certificate checking
MUST use the exact rationals, never the rounded display.

**Certificate binding.** The estimate's certificate MUST bind
object, interface, closure, target and effective schedule hashes, root
variant/controls, accuracy, expansion identity and the resolved
`bindings.invocation` (§12.3). Even a default-only realisation has an
explicit empty-entries schedule document with those bindings, hence a
schedule hash. Its root region has `estimate: { precision_digits: 34,
exact_resources: { "<encoded row name>": { numerator: "<integer>",
denominator: "<positive integer>" } } }`; fractions are reduced,
nonnegative and decimal-string encoded, zero is 0/1. `resources` uses
the identical encoded keys and canonical decimal strings returned by
`estimate_get`. A checker MUST rebuild the bound realisation, recompute
all exact rows and their upward decimal encodings, and reject any
binding, key, rational or display mismatch. A producer's resource map
alone is not verification.

**LAUNCH-ESTIMATE witness**

```json
{
  "variant":"forward","inherited_controls":0,
  "binding": {"args":["2"],"budget":["2","0","0","0"]},
  "precision_digits": 34,
  "classes": ["A","B]","idle"],
  "live": [
    {"class":"A","count":2,"start":"0","end":"1/3"},
    {"class":"B]","count":1,"start":"0","end":"1/3"}
  ],
  "rows": [
    ["peak_qubits[A]","2"],
    ["peak_qubits[B%5D]","1"],
    ["peak_qubits[idle]","0"],
    ["qubit_second[A]","0.6666666666666666666666666666666667"],
    ["qubit_second[B%5D]","0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"],
    ["qubit_second[idle]","0"],
    ["time","0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"]
  ],
  "decimal_checks": [
    {"rational":"0","decimal":"0"},
    {"rational":"2","decimal":"2"},
    {"rational":"1/8","decimal":"0.125"},
    {"rational":"1/3","decimal":"0.3333333333333333333333333333333334"},
    {"rational":"2/3","decimal":"0.6666666666666666666666666666666667"},
    {"rational":"1/30","decimal":"0.03333333333333333333333333333333334"},
    {"rational":"10/3","decimal":"3.333333333333333333333333333333334"}
  ]
}
```

This **aggregation witness** assumes the displayed scheduled intervals,
unit class rates and no other events; it does not assert an unbound
oracle gate count. A permitted cost expression `1 / 3` produces that
makespan. The exact class peaks are 2 and 1, not a replacement scalar 3;
occupancies are 2/3 and 1/3, and the idle class is present. Changing the
argument `2` to `1` denotes a different request, even at the same budget.
No 0.8 request can transmit that distinction through §10's signature.

# bennett-dialect-spec

The LLVM dialect contract of the Bennett ecosystem: the marker-function ABI (names, signatures, positive legality requirement), portable handle convention, outlining / budget / region well-formedness conventions, materialised-normalisation rules, the residual-poison validator, and the legality conformance corpus.

It also carries a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model, so a competing plugin can be conformance-tested at spec level.

Depends on: `bennett-isa-spec` (its reference semantics targets the ISA formal model).

Layout:

- `spec/`: normative text. [`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md`](spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) is the current draft (version in its title; 0.2.12 at time of writing, accepted by the sol seat in round 11), `bennett_markers.h` the marker ABI header, `errata-0.2.10.md` binding interpretations of accepted 0.2.10. See [`spec/README.md`](spec/README.md) for the change rules.
- `corpus/legality/`: legality corpus.
- `validator/`: Rust crate `bennett-dialect-validator`.
- `scripts/`: the dialect checker (`dialect_check_v02.py`) and `pre-push`, which gates a clean worktree of the pushed commit (cargo test in `validator/` plus the checker).
- `emission/`, `docs/`: emission notes and audit/brief records.
- `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`: ISA version this repo is graded against (a test pins its current value; see HANDOFF.md for what is current).

Current state and open consumer blockers: [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md).
# Bennett LLVM Dialect — next draft — OUTLINE 0.3

**Date:** 2026-10-31. **Status:** proposed outline for maintainer rulings; no draft text and no ratification implied. Prepared as the design sub-agent. Accepted baseline: dialect **0.2.10**, ISA **0.8**. Candidate target: ISA **0.9 draft**, accepted by sol/codex seats, muse held, K64/K65 awaiting maintainer ratification. This outline follows the structure and ruling convention of `outline-0.2.md`, while preserving the accepted text's subsequent council repairs (notably Appendix A).

**Scope:** all 40 open children of `b-dialect-spec-8wf`, the three requested dependency beads bz6/cbe/0t9, nonblocking PT12 bead 0y2, and the additionally relevant open ISA-wording bead gwf: **45 item records, 22 decisions**. The epic itself remains the scope ledger. Other open corpus/tooling siblings are not implicitly completed by this design.

**Reading key.** DLT IDs name `../bennett-conformance/clauses/DLT-0.2.10.statements.md`; ISA IDs name `ISA-0.9-draft.statements.md` there. An unsuffixed section or paragraph is a clause dependency; `.S<n>` selects a statement. K/E numbers are ISA §0 change-row mnemonics, not dependency IDs. NS is `../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md` iteration7, especially §3, §6.1 and the entire §8 ladder. Audit row IDs retain their original 0.1 meanings. “0.1 capability” below means the north-star capability the 0.1 narrowing removed or omitted, not a claim that restrictive 0.1 already implemented it. Every item distinguishes that history from the actual 0.2.10 state.

## Part A. Draft structure and release boundaries

Proposed section plan: retain §§1–25 and Appendices B/C; add an opening change ledger and dependency declarations; update §§3.4,5,6,8.4,9.5,11.5,17.5–17.6,19.9,23,25 for ISA 0.9; preserve the accepted normalisation/map unless an item explicitly changes it. Appendix A becomes a provenance ledger of departures from ratified outlines, retaining all accepted council repairs. There is no proposed change to marker ABI signatures or descriptor version2 in this outline.

Proposed editorial/process sentences (for the eventual draft, subject to D01):

- The next draft MUST preserve every north-star capability and every existing non-narrowing construction, with deferral limited to mechanics and any real missing ISA operation named precisely as a blocker. [deps: DLT-front-P3, DLT-1.3, DLT-1.6, DLT-24, DLT-25.1]
- Every new or changed normative sentence MUST end with a dependency declaration naming the DLT or ISA statement or clause IDs on which it relies. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-23]
- The 0.2.11 compatible track MUST retain the ISA 0.8 target, existing marker signatures, canonical map and externally visible reference behaviour while clarifying inherited rules and supplying their missing closure evidence. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
- The 0.3 track MUST declare ISA 0.9 and its complete sub-version tuple, regenerate affected objects, canonical hashes, plugin identity, module flag, schedules and certificates, and preserve the accepted 0.2.10 ISA 0.8 artefacts as historical versioned vectors. [deps: DLT-16.2-P2, DLT-19.9, DLT-23, ISA-front-P2, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3]

The dependency-tag process rule additionally comes directly from repo CLAUDE.md rule12; its dependency marker above names the existing notation/versioning context, not an assertion that 0.2.10 already had rule12. Same principle for the no-narrowing standing rule (repo rule10, NS thesis).

Three groups below describe landing mechanics, never reduced scope:

- **0.2.11:** compatible/editorial confirmations and analysis clarifications already required by 0.2.10; these are mostly open-bead closure/evidence work, not newly invented capabilities.
- **0.3:** actual ISA 0.9 adoption, dynamic-register/token/R lowering and removal of obsolete dependency verdicts.
- **ISA-blocked:** bz6/cbe remain external adoption/implementation blockers in the accepted baseline, destined for 0.3 once the publication and execution gates pass; 0.9 contains their missing sentences already. This is not a claim of an additional ISA design gap.

## Part C — 0.2.11 item records

<a id="i01"></a>
### I01 — Taint-driven lifting

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.1`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D08. **Audit:** 1.4-d, D17, B-31, B-50, O-05.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 ran the classical part of ctrl/adj/compute once without controlling its escaping stores; the promised capability is inferred coherent execution of ordinary code.

**0.2.10 delta:** Already restored by DLT-7.1-P7 and DLT-10-P2 through DLT-10-P45: Cl/M/Co state, classical twins, coherent slices, store events and clean control workspace; no surviving generation-only restriction.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.7-P1, ISA-5.2, ISA-5.3 and ISA-6 provide controlled oracle calls, total XOR outcomes and cleanup; no extra ISA sentence is needed.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> An action body containing classical code MUST retain the Cl/M/Co partition, classical twins, predicated outliving stores and clean-workspace obligations of TDL-1 through TDL-12. [deps: DLT-10, DLT-18.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2, ISA-6]

**Rejected alternatives:** Generation-only execution changes the controlled store result; retaining incidental garbage as undeclared outputs violates the accepted cleanup contract.

**Conformance consequence:** DT/DS: retain tdl-a through tdl-d, co-loop-identity, co-loop-adj-overwritten, co-observe-write-observe and deferred-channel cases; add no subset rejection. Rungs 4/7/8 retain explicit-oracle paths.

**Dependency order:** I07, I17, I22, I33; I31 is the nested reversible-call case.

**Maintainer choice:** D08 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i03"></a>
### I03 — Parametrised inplace

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.3`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D14. **Audit:** 1.4-f / ISA-3.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 inplace accepted only x, omitting preserved quantum inputs, memory and classical parameters.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-8.7-P3, DLT-8.7-P4, DLT-8.7-P5 already support all bindings by specialisation, library actions and the CC oracle/swap/inverse expansion; BLK-3 is retired in DLT-25.1-P1.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K4: ISA-4.5-P5 now provides native qinplace with ins/mem/args and an inverse-pair entry; K62/63 pin adjoints and call-edge identities. This is an optional lowering, not a newly missing capability.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Parametrised in-place calls MUST preserve the existing CC expansion for every admitted pair. [deps: DLT-8.7-P5, DLT-8.7-P6, ISA-3.3-P9, ISA-4.7]

Optional 0.3 lowering, after D01:

> Native parametrised qinplace MAY replace that expansion only when its covering ISA inverse-pair entry is verified. [deps: DLT-8.7-P5, DLT-8.7-P6, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.3]

**Rejected alternatives:** Reinstating BLK-3 is false; replacing every pair with native qinplace would exclude the sampled path described in I14.

**Conformance consequence:** DO/DM/DS: compare controlled CC expansion and native K4 for exhaustive/library pairs, both orientations, changed preserved input and too-small budget; ISA F checks signature/certificate admission.

**Dependency order:** I06, I17, I14; D01 gates native 0.9 objects and scheduled expansions.

**Maintainer choice:** D14 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i04"></a>
### I04 — Allocation classes

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.4`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D10. **Audit:** D1, B-05.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 rejected cls != 0 despite classed allocations in the north star.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-3.4-P18 already defines the link-order class-name table; DLT-3.4-P22 dispatches it.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-3.3-P8, ISA-4.3 and ISA-8.3 supply classed allocations and target classes; no gap.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Allocation class zero MUST denote any, and positive class indices MUST select the existing link-order class-name table. [deps: DLT-3.4-P18, DLT-3.4-P22, ISA-3.3-P8, ISA-8.3]

**Rejected alternatives:** Accepting only class zero or merging classes without names loses the requested resource class.

**Conformance consequence:** DA/DB/DL: retain operand-class and class-table ordering/load-error cases; DS checks actual class allocation.

**Dependency order:** I18; I19 only changes width realisation.

**Maintainer choice:** D10 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i05"></a>
### I05 — Per-region budget tables

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.5`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D02. **Audit:** D2.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 offered only B_default, requiring source annotations or no per-region memory budgets.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-9.2-P2 through P8 already define defaults, region-key/glob precedence, one-shot selection_identity overlay, frozen capacities and final-object tokens.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-5.4-P2, ISA-5.4-P3 and ISA-10 provide the four-component launch tuple; K3 preserves tuple identity at device minting; no table instruction is missing.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Per-region budget resolution MUST retain the staged baseline, frozen layout, single selection_identity overlay and final-identity token binding, with explicit non-sentinel marker fields applied after lookup. [deps: DLT-9.1-P7, DLT-9.2-P4, DLT-9.2-P5, DLT-9.2-P6, DLT-9.2-P7, DLT-9.2-P8, ISA-5.4-P3]

**Rejected alternatives:** Iterating the final hash back into lookup creates cycles; steps-only records or source edits would restore the narrowing.

**Conformance consequence:** DD/DM: retain budget-stage including H16/H32 and multiple shapes; check tuple/hash change separately. DX4/7/8 use out-of-band records.

**Dependency order:** I33 before table users; feeds I20, I07, I17 and I40.

**Maintainer choice:** D02 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i06"></a>
### I06 — Classical oracle arguments

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.6`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D03. **Audit:** D3.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 required front ends to specialise constants into a scalar oracle rather than passing fixed-at-entry classical values.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-8.2-P3 and DLT-8.4-P1 already pass ARG carrier bits, excluding their values from shape identity; DLT-16.2-P11 maps args.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.7-P1.S5/S6/S7 and ISA-3.7 explicitly forward classical/token parameters, and ISA-12.3-P2 binds exact argument bits; no gap.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> ARG bindings MUST carry the caller's fixed-at-entry values in parameter order without specialising their values into the shape, and pointer arguments MUST retain entry-state semantics. [deps: DLT-8.2-P3, DLT-8.4-P1, DLT-8.5, ISA-4.7-P1, ISA-12.3-P2]

**Rejected alternatives:** Packing all inputs or constant-specialising every runtime argument imposes source/ABI restrictions the accepted descriptor removed.

**Conformance consequence:** DO/DM: two invocations of one shape with different ARG bits and pointer-rich entry state; mutate params_hash to ensure replay refuses the wrong binding. DX4 uses classical database length.

**Dependency order:** I17, I40; unchanged marker ABI.

**Maintainer choice:** D03 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i07"></a>
### I07 — Full lifted LLVM closure

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.7`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D04. **Audit:** D4, B-39.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 BD-E-LIFT-SUBSET excluded memory, calls, FP and vectors, including ordinary SQLite query paths.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-16.1-P1 deletes the diagnostic and admits every materialised function; DLT-12.2-P38/C enumerate 546 generic intrinsics, and DLT-16.4 maps memory/calls/aggregates/carriers.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.2, ISA-3.7 and ISA-7.2 provide memory, allocation, lproc recursion and lifted control; E17.1 has no location-width cap, but the existing canonical dialect limb map remains valid.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Every materialised function in an oracle closure MUST remain liftable through the complete normalisation and mapping tables, including memory, recursion, indirect calls, vectors and floating-point carriers. [deps: DLT-12.2, DLT-C, DLT-16.1-P1, DLT-16.4, ISA-3.7, ISA-4.2, ISA-7.2]

**Rejected alternatives:** A scalar-only profile, silently trapping an unsupported generic intrinsic, or limiting the contract to checker seeds narrows scope.

**Conformance consequence:** DN/DM/DV/DR/DF/DX: retain all N0–N35 and V1–V16 rows; memory/recursive/wide/vector/FP/varargs witnesses, native comparison and prefix reversal; SQLite full closure is the rung-4 gate, not a mini substitute.

**Dependency order:** I33, I17, I22/I35, I15, I37, I12, I36; map after normalisation.

**Maintainer choice:** D04 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i08"></a>
### I08 — Requested deferred measurement

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.8`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D09. **Audit:** D5, B-36.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 refused measurement under unitary regions without the front-end opt-in promised by the north star.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-7.5 and DLT-18.2 already specify deferred channel records, placement under standalone adjoint/compute and a declared observation boundary; default refusal remains intentional.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.4, ISA-4.6 and ISA-12.1 provide coherent copies, predicates and channel claims; K64/65 matter only when scheduled support/cleanup points are bound.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Front-end-requested deferred measurement MUST retain its channel records through the declared observation boundary, including the accepted standalone-adjoint and compute placement rules. [deps: DLT-7.5, DLT-18.2, ISA-4.4, ISA-4.6, ISA-12.1]

**Rejected alternatives:** Measuring immediately changes the controlled instrument; uncopying retained channel records silently turns the channel into a unitary.

**Conformance consequence:** DT/DS: retain deferred-channel-adj-place and compute/loop boundary cases, both control values and superposition; refusal without opt-in remains a negative.

**Dependency order:** I01; I-gwf for certificate record, D01 for scheduled claims.

**Maintainer choice:** D09 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i09"></a>
### I09 — Borrowing outside generations

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.9`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D11. **Audit:** D6, B-49.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 used T-BORROW outside a generation.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-18.3 already deletes T-BORROW and uses clean allocation outside statically verified borrow frames.
