spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 3.1 Reserved names

Every function or global name that starts with `__bennett_` is reserved. The reserved names
other than the markers of §3.2 are:

| Prefix | Who defines | Who may reference it |
|---|---|---|
| `__bennett_nz_` | production: nobody; comparison-only native support exception (§17.7); **primitives**, lowered by the map (§16.4) and the CC/R lowering (§17.4) | the normaliser (§12) and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_sf_` | `bennett-arith` bitcode (§13) | the normaliser and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_rt_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode (§11, §14, §17, §20); the driver's layout objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>` (§14.9) and comparison-only `__bennett_rt_native_layout` (§19.5) | the normaliser, the driver's synthesised code (§19) and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_dev_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode: device-model entry points and device state (§11.3) | the plugin's L lowering of effect markers and runtimes bitcode |

The primitives are exactly:

```
declare void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 %code)    ; fault with code C (§12.3)
declare ptr  @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64 %n)       ; ISA alloc / lift.alloc (§17.1)
declare void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr %p)        ; ISA free / lift.free
```

A **producer input** is a bitcode file given to the driver other than `bennett-runtimes` and
`bennett-arith` bitcode. A producer input:

1. MUST NOT define (`define`) a function whose name is a marker or a reserved name
   (`BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED`);
2. MUST NOT declare, define or reference a reserved name that is not a marker
   (`BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN`);
3. MUST NOT use a marker other than as the callee of a call or invoke (`BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS`):
   no stored, compared, passed or cast marker address, so that no marker is ever called
   indirectly.

The driver checks rules 1–3 on every producer input before linking; the plugin checks rules 1
and 3 on the linked module, and, in closed-unit mode (§19.7), all three on its input. Checked
examples are producer inputs. `__bennett_ctrl_v`, the variadic probe of the spike ABI v0, is
not a marker; it is rejected by rule 2. *(non-normative)* The legality corpus's trace runtime
defines markers; it is a test harness, never a production link input (§19.9).



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
### 3.1 Reserved names

Every function or global name that starts with `__bennett_` is reserved. The reserved names
other than the markers of §3.2 are:

| Prefix | Who defines | Who may reference it |
|---|---|---|
| `__bennett_nz_` | production: nobody; comparison-only native support exception (§17.7); **primitives**, lowered by the map (§16.4) and the CC/R lowering (§17.4) | the normaliser (§12) and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_sf_` | `bennett-arith` bitcode (§13) | the normaliser and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_rt_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode (§11, §14, §17, §20); the driver's layout objects `__bennett_rt_blk.<f>` (§14.9) and comparison-only `__bennett_rt_native_layout` (§19.5); the plugin's private `__bennett_rt_qtab` (§6.5, §19.5) | the normaliser, the plugin's CC/R lowering, the driver's synthesised code (§19) and runtimes bitcode |
| `__bennett_dev_` | `bennett-runtimes` bitcode: device-model entry points and device state (§11.3) | the plugin's L lowering of effect markers and runtimes bitcode |

The primitives are exactly:

```
declare void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64 %code)    ; fault with code C (§12.3)
declare ptr  @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64 %n)       ; ISA alloc / lift.alloc (§17.1)
declare void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr %p)        ; ISA free / lift.free
```

A **producer input** is a bitcode file given to the driver other than `bennett-runtimes` and
`bennett-arith` bitcode. A producer input:

1. MUST NOT define (`define`) a function whose name is a marker or a reserved name
   (`BD-E-MARKER-DEFINED`);
2. MUST NOT declare, define or reference a reserved name that is not a marker
   (`BD-E-MARKER-UNKNOWN`);
3. MUST NOT use a marker other than as the callee of a call or invoke (`BD-E-MARKER-ADDRESS`):
   no stored, compared, passed or cast marker address, so that no marker is ever called
   indirectly.

The driver checks rules 1–3 on every producer input before linking; the plugin checks rules 1
and 3 on the linked module, and, in closed-unit mode (§19.7), all three on its input. Checked
examples are producer inputs. `__bennett_ctrl_v`, the variadic probe of the spike ABI v0, is
not a marker; it is rejected by rule 2. *(non-normative)* The legality corpus's trace runtime
defines markers; it is a test harness, never a production link input (§19.9).



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 16.3 Names and layout

| LLVM entity | ISA name |
|---|---|
| the i-th parameter of g | `a<i>` (fields of a flattened aggregate `a<i>_<f>`) |
| the k-th instruction of g that produces a value (0-based, in layout order, phis included) | location `v<k>` (fields of an aggregate value `v<k>_<f>`) |
| the j-th constant materialisation of g, in text order of the output | location `c<j>` |
| the j-th mapping temporary of g, in text order | location `t<j>` |
| the i-th basic block of g (0-based, layout order) | block `^b<i>` |
| a block created by a compare tree in block i (§16.4), in creation order | `^b<i>.s<j>` (j ≥ 1) |
| for the q-th indirect call of block i (q ≥ 1): the leaf of candidate k (sorted order), the default, the continuation | `^b<i>.q<q>c<k>`, `^b<i>.q<q>d`, `^b<i>.q<q>r` |
| the edge block from block i to block s | `^b<i>.e<s>` |
| the n-th global of the layout (1-based, in link order) | data symbol and global `G<n>` |

The `regs` line lists every location of the function that is neither a parameter nor the result,
in order of first appearance as a destination in the text, as `name:reg[W]`, separated by `, `;
it is omitted when empty. W: a value's width (`i1` → 1, `ptr addrspace(a)` → P(a)); a constant's width; a
temporary's width (1 for a compare, else its operation's width).

**Layout.** The reference object's `layout` is the device link layout (§19.5) restricted to the
non-arena globals that the L closure names in `lift.addr` and, transitively, non-arena globals their initialisers
point to (in link order, numbered G1, G2, …), with the link's `ptr_width`, `null_size`,
`heap_base`, `heap_limit_max`, `H`, `A_tab` and `S_cells`, and `interface_regions` = { "ref": the
shape's regions }. An arena-relocated global has no G<n> symbol or data image:
its address is @coh plus its arena offset. `coh` is placed at A_coh (§19.5), disjoint
from globals and heap. Bases of all other regions follow `<mems>` order: each base is the smallest multiple of
the region's size that is ≥ the end of the previous range (the heap's end `heap_base +
heap_limit_max` for the first when there is no arena; otherwise the arena's end). H is the smallest value with every range inside [0, 2^H); A_tab
is the smallest value with 2^A_tab ≥ P + `heap_limit_max`, P the link's number of globals plus
the largest number of interface regions of any oracle, so that allocation-table overflow never
precedes heap exhaustion (both fault with code 2, ISA §4.2, and a reference object with fewer
pre-entries than the device object faults at the same allocation); S_cells is the smallest value
with 2^S_cells ≥ the frozen `stack` capacity of §9.2. These link capacities are
frozen before provisional identity construction and include unmatched raw entries
and static non-sentinel record fields; identity overlays never recompute them.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md
### 16.3 Names and layout

| LLVM entity | ISA name |
|---|---|
| the i-th parameter of g | `a<i>` (fields of a flattened aggregate `a<i>_<f>`) |
| the k-th instruction of g that produces a value (0-based, in layout order, phis included) | location `v<k>` (fields of an aggregate value `v<k>_<f>`) |
| the j-th constant materialisation of g, in text order of the output | location `c<j>` |
| the j-th mapping temporary of g, in text order | location `t<j>` |
| the i-th basic block of g (0-based, layout order) | block `^b<i>` |
| a block created by a compare tree in block i (§16.4), in creation order | `^b<i>.s<j>` (j ≥ 1) |
| for the q-th indirect call of block i (q ≥ 1): the leaf of candidate k (sorted order), the default, the continuation | `^b<i>.q<q>c<k>`, `^b<i>.q<q>d`, `^b<i>.q<q>r` |
| the edge block from block i to block s | `^b<i>.e<s>` |
| the n-th global of the layout (1-based, in link order) | data symbol and global `G<n>` |

The `regs` line lists every location of the function that is neither a parameter nor the result,
in order of first appearance as a destination in the text, as `name:reg[W]`, separated by `, `;
it is omitted when empty. W: a value's width (`i1` → 1, `ptr addrspace(a)` → P(a)); a constant's width; a
temporary's width (1 for a compare, else its operation's width).

**Layout.** The reference object's `layout` is the device link layout (§19.5) restricted to the
non-arena globals that the L closure names in `lift.addr` and, transitively, non-arena globals their initialisers
point to (in link order, numbered G1, G2, …), with the link's `ptr_width`, `null_size`,
`heap_base`, `heap_limit_max`, `H`, `A_tab` and `S_cells`, and `interface_regions` = { "ref": the
shape's regions }. An arena-relocated global has no G<n> symbol or data image:
its address is @coh plus its arena offset. All bases, including `coh`, other shape regions
and the private qreg table, are resolved once by §19.5 and retained when restricting the
layout; no reference object repacks them. H is the smallest value with every range of the
complete device link layout inside [0, 2^H), including `__bennett_rt_qtab` when present; A_tab
is the smallest value with 2^A_tab ≥ P + `heap_limit_max`, P the link's number of non-arena globals (including the shadow and private qreg table when present) plus
the largest number of interface regions of any oracle, so that allocation-table overflow never
precedes heap exhaustion (both fault with code 2, ISA §4.2, and a reference object with fewer
pre-entries than the device object faults at the same allocation); S_cells is the smallest value
with 2^S_cells ≥ the frozen `stack` capacity of §9.2. These link capacities are
frozen before provisional identity construction and include unmatched raw entries
and static non-sentinel record fields; identity overlays never recompute them.



spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md
### 16.9 Example: rung 2 (heap, phis, a loop, `free`)

A linked list of n nodes built with `malloc` (a specialised 16-byte-call witness of the runtimes allocator over `lift.alloc`, shown with
its code; the general C wrapper is specified in §17.1), summed and freed. n comes from a 2-wire `IN` register zero-extended to `i8`:

```llvm lift=listsum region=@listsum desc=IN(2,zext);tgt=8
define ptr @malloc(i64 %n) {
  %p = call ptr @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64 %n)
  ret ptr %p
}

define void @free(ptr %p) {
  %z = icmp eq ptr %p, null
  br i1 %z, label %out, label %rel

rel:
  call void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr %p)
  br label %out

out:
  ret void
}

define internal i8 @listsum(i8 %n) {
b0:
  %z = icmp eq i8 %n, 0
  br i1 %z, label %b3, label %b1

b1:
  %i = phi i8 [ 0, %b0 ], [ %i2, %b1 ]
  %head = phi ptr [ null, %b0 ], [ %node, %b1 ]
  %node = call ptr @malloc(i64 16)
  store i8 %i, ptr %node
  %np = getelementptr i8, ptr %node, i64 8
  store ptr %head, ptr %np
  %i2 = add i8 %i, 1
  %more = icmp ult i8 %i2, %n
  br i1 %more, label %b1, label %b2

b2:
  %p = phi ptr [ %node, %b1 ], [ %next, %b2 ]
  %s = phi i8 [ 0, %b1 ], [ %s2, %b2 ]
  %v = load i8, ptr %p
  %s2 = add i8 %s, %v
  %nextp = getelementptr i8, ptr %p, i64 8
  %next = load ptr, ptr %nextp
  call void @free(ptr %p)
  %end = icmp eq ptr %next, null
  br i1 %end, label %b4, label %b2

b3:
  ret i8 0

b4:
  ret i8 %s2
}
```

```text linkmap=listsum
null_size=4096 heap_base=4096 heap_limit_max=64 globals=none budget-table: largest stack 512, largest heap 64
```

A_tab = 6 (2^6 ≥ 0 + 64), H = 13 (4096 + 64 ≤ 2^13), S_cells = 9:


### 16.9 Example: rung 2 (heap, phis, a loop, `free`)

A linked list of n nodes built with `malloc` (a specialised 16-byte-call witness of the runtimes allocator over `lift.alloc`, shown with

# 2cg — distinguish exact primitive history from a minimal C-wrapper fixture

Council proposal/disposition for `b-dialect-spec-2cg` (dialect repository).

## Round-1 blocking items and resolution

Read the [round-1 report](../../../../bennett/docs/council/pass2-2cg-2026-11-02/round-1/report.md)
and both `out-sol.json` and `out-codex.json` in that directory. The table
maps every blocking item to this revision; it claims a proposed resolution,
not a new council verdict.

| Item | Change and witness |
|---|---|
| sol B1; codex B1 — allocator allowance lacks mixed-object applicability | Add only the fat-object paragraph below to §17.1 of both dialect 0.2 and 0.3 texts. Witness: native main keeps host-only work/libc native; the marked allocator-using device closure and its native comparison execute the stated bookkeeping, transferring shared metadata under §8.5 and addresses under §§19.4–19.5. |

## Defect and North Star scope

Both maintained dialect texts, §17.1 “C allocation alignment”, require
ISA §7.1's exact primitive bump, requested-size record and nonreclaiming
history. C payload extents are ABI-rounded; alignment padding is explicit
and minimal. Neither clause publishes a runtime query for old requested
sizes/liveness. The bead asks whether the exact three-record
`scripts/0212_allocation.c` witness also limits every production allocator.

The actual fixture calls itself an **independent primitive/wrapper trace
fixture, not a production allocator**. Runtime ordinary-bitcode bodies
are materialised on both sides (dialect §§17.7/19.1). On that reading,
ordinary charged bookkeeping is already expressible. This is a genuine
wording/conformance-boundary ambiguity worth resolving, **not evidence
that a new ISA allocation-query instruction is necessary**, and not a
remaining unimplemented realloc in the originating runtime. Its later
source/request supersedes the earlier blocked-query interpretation.

North Star §§3.2/5/6.1 and §8 rungs 4–5 requires real libc, unmodified
SQLite and allocator-based Julia collections. A fixed record cap,
native-only allocation query, copying past the old payload or dropping
realloc would narrow scope. Ordinary dynamic metadata preserves it;
actual added budget/history costs must remain visible and reversible.

## Alternatives and worked witness

The originating note inspected runtimes `.tmp/sol-runtimes-queue/cross-repo-beads.md`,
“resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping”, its implementation update,
and “wider allocator re-pin”; `src/allocator/bump.c` read-only. That reported body keeps requested sizes/live records in explicit 32-byte
heap records, reuses freed metadata slots as data, and uses an explicit
zero-size primitive probe before alignment. It does not reclaim payload
addresses or access the ISA's private allocation table.

With heap base 4096, alignment 16 and an initial raw `alloc(3)`:

| Program/body | Subsequent primitive requests | Payload base | Final bump | Records including raw alloc |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| Minimal fixture `cmalloc(1)` | 13 padding, 16 payload | 4112 | 4128 | 3 |
| Inspected production `malloc(1)`, empty metadata pool | 0 probe, 12 padding, 16 payload, 32 metadata | 4112 | 4160 | 5 |

The zero-size primitive advances one byte but records requested size 0
(ISA §7.1). The production body therefore needs 64 heap bytes in this
fresh case, rather than 32. With a 32-byte heap its metadata allocation
faults after the successful probe/padding/payload prefix; ordinary
function calls do not imply transaction-wide rollback. Reversal restores
the prefix's records and stores by the ordinary reversible history.

The old-size information witness is also valid: allocations 16 then 32,
or 32 then 16, have the same first pointer p and final bump p+48. A
cursor alone cannot tell whether `realloc(p,48)` preserves 16 or 32
bytes. This proves a need to retain sizes, not a need for a privileged
query: a side list written at each allocation supplies the information.

| Alternative | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Treat the minimal fixture as the only allowed production trace | Requires another published metadata mechanism to retain full libc scope. |
| Publish a new allocation-size/liveness primitive | Possible larger contract change; unnecessary for the inspected implementation. |
| Permit ordinary explicit charged bookkeeping | Keeps exact primitive semantics and native/device equality. Recommended. |

## Recommended exact spec diff

In **both** maintained maps append to §17.1 after the allocation vector:

```text
The allocation vector fixes the trace of its stated thin wrapper,
not a universal trace for every implementation of malloc/calloc/realloc.
Runtime bodies may retain requested size, liveness and alignment state
in ordinary materialised memory, including dynamically allocated metadata
and explicit zero-size address probes. Every such operation, its stores,
padding and allocation history is present identically in device and
native materialisation and charged to the actual heap/step/table budgets.
The C payload request retains the rounded extent and returned-base rule
above; padding immediately before that payload is minimal from the
actual current primitive bump. No hidden header, private ISA-table query,
native-only map or fixed metadata cap is implied or permitted by this
allowance. Reusing live metadata storage as ordinary data does not reclaim
primitive payload addresses or allocation-table/history entries.

realloc preserves min(old requested size,new size) bytes of a live C
allocation, using retained ordinary metadata or another published
mechanism. Invalid/dead/interior pointers retain the materialised
memory/fault contract. Overflow preflights precede the corresponding
allocation mutation; a later primitive failure retains the successfully
executed ordinary prefix unless explicitly reversed. Both comparison
builds execute the same body, including its failure prefix. Tests of a
production runtime bind that body's identity and exact resulting trace;
they do not impose the thin fixture's record count on a different body.
```

Keep the three-record fixture and its output exactly as published. Add a
separate tagged production-body vector to the dialect/runtime acceptance
corpus, not a replacement that weakens primitive history checks. This
proposal grants no blanket memory-check exemption to runtime functions.

## Fat-object applicability

Incorporate [the shared scoping statement](fat-object-scope.md#citable-scoping-statement).
Append the following proposed normative paragraph to §17.1 in BOTH spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md (0.2) and spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec-0.3-draft.md (0.3):

```text
In a mixed fat object, this bookkeeping allowance governs the allocator
closure selected for a marked device entry and that closure's native
comparison build. The surrounding native host binary, native entry,
argv/envp and host-only libc remain outside that device object, subject
to the existing placement/promotion rules of §§4.2–4.4 and 19.4. Allocation
traces, budgets and runtime-body identities bind that selected closure.
Shared caller allocation state, including ordinary allocator metadata
bytes and primitive allocation history, is transferred and charged under
§8.5; boundary addresses and layout follow §§19.4–19.5. Neither a
whole-program device entry nor a new allocation query is required by
this clarification.
```

The shared statement lists today's assumptions and the exact remaining
container, launch, budget, handle, tape and host-memory contract changes.
This note does not assume that the device object is the entire binary.

## Hashes, catalog and consumers

ISA primitive semantics and ref catalog bytes stay fixed. Different
materialised runtime bodies change bitcode/native manifests, dialect
materialisation identity, generated ISA closures, resource costs and
consumer pins; exact addresses may change too. A dialect wording revision
must obey its full-version/mapping policy. Driver/plugin/conformance must
re-pin and budget the actual body, not transplant minimal fixture costs.
No sibling publication or tracker database is written here.

## Conformance vectors and verification

Check both traces above, min(16,48)=16 versus min(32,48)=32, shrink,
null/zero-size, dead/interior pointer, direct intervening primitive alloc,
pool-slot reuse, first stronger alignment and failures at each primitive.
Reverse all actual metadata/list stores and primitive records and replay
identically; never test only the returned payload. Exact hand arithmetic
is checked by [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py). The runtime's reported LLVM18/23 gates
were read as originating evidence, not rerun here; this job runs no
full sibling gate and claims no SQLite/Julia rung acceptance.

## Release and route

Recommend a **current dialect §17.1 clarification**, in the 0.2 and 0.3
maps under their publication rules; no ISA 0.9 K or ISA 0.10 dependency.
Retain related jjr/0na and re-pin work with their owners as appropriate.
The orchestrator can attach this note to 2cg; no new cross-repo bead is
needed because the decision is already tracked there.

## Publication and validation boundary

This is a revision proposal, not council acceptance or a consumer implementation.
Apply the additional applicability paragraph to §17.1 in both dialect maps;
the earlier allocator clarification and vectors above are retained unchanged.
The dialect's spec/README.md and §23 require a full version bump for semantic
changes and council review. This note grants no in-version publication
exception. Mapping/body/layout changes require the corresponding identity
and consumer-pin updates. There is no new ISA primitive or catalog dependency;
existing version-specific fault/reversal dependencies remain with their
owners. This revision edits neither dialect text nor its examples.

The reproducible finite and document checks are in [check_rev2.py](check_rev2.py):
`timeout 120 python3 -I -B /home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec/docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2/check_rev2.py`.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Read-only finite witnesses for the rev2 proposals, not an ISA/format checker.

Uses only this repository's published catalog routine/data and the eight notes.
No subprocesses, network, git, temporary files, tracker or source mutations.
"""
from pathlib import Path
from fractions import Fraction as F
from copy import deepcopy
from math import isqrt, ceil, floor
import hashlib, json, re
ROOT=Path('/home/tobias/Projects/bennett-workspace/bennett-isa-spec')
OUT=ROOT/'docs/notes/2026-11-02-rev2'
text=(OUT/'fd0.md').read_text()
entry=json.loads(text.split('<!-- import-example -->\n```json\n',1)[1].split('\n```',1)[0])
m=entry['model']
def q(x):
    assert isinstance(x,str)
    v=F(x)
    assert str(v)==x, ('noncanonical rational',x)
    return v
def dims(d):return {k:q(v) for k,v in d.items() if q(v)}
def dimcombine(a,b,sign=1):
    out=dict(a)
    for k,v in b.items():out[k]=out.get(k,F(0))+sign*v
    return {k:v for k,v in out.items() if v}
def unit(n):
    u=m['units'][n]
    assert set(u)=={'dimension','scale'} and q(u['scale'])>0
    return q(u['scale']),dims(u['dimension'])
class Unbound(Exception):pass

def ev(e,params,seen=()):
    if isinstance(e,str):return q(e),{}
    assert isinstance(e,dict)
    if set(e)=={'lit','unit'}:
        scale,d=unit(e['unit']); return q(e['lit'])*scale,d
    if set(e)=={'param'}:
        p=m['parameters'][e['param']]; x=params[e['param']]
        if x is None:raise Unbound(e['param'])
        v,d=ev(x,{},seen); assert not d
        scale,d=unit(p['unit']); return v*scale,d
    if set(e)=={'quantity'}:
        n=e['quantity']; assert n not in seen, ('cycle',n)
        v,d=ev(m['quantities'][n]['expr'],params,seen+(n,))
        assert d==unit(m['quantities'][n]['unit'])[1]
        return v,d
    if set(e)=={'external'}:raise Unbound(e['external']['location'])
    assert set(e)=={'op','args'}
    op=e['op']; a=e['args']
    assert op in ['+','-','*','/','min','max','ceil','sqrt','pow']
    assert len(a)==(1 if op in ['ceil','sqrt'] else 2)
    x,dx=ev(a[0],params,seen)
    if op=='ceil':assert not dx; return F(ceil(x)),{}
    if op=='sqrt':
        assert x>=0
        n,d=isqrt(x.numerator),isqrt(x.denominator)
        assert n*n==x.numerator and d*d==x.denominator, 'nonrational root: use exact algebraic AST/enclosure'
        return F(n,d),{k:v/2 for k,v in dx.items()}
    y,dy=ev(a[1],params,seen)
    if op in ['+','-','min','max']:
        assert dx==dy, ('unit mismatch',dx,dy)
        return {'+':lambda:x+y,'-':lambda:x-y,'min':lambda:min(x,y),'max':lambda:max(x,y)}[op](),dx
    if op=='*':return x*y,dimcombine(dx,dy)
    if op=='/':assert y!=0; return x/y,dimcombine(dx,dy,-1)
    assert not dy and y.denominator==1
    assert x!=0 or y>0
    return x**int(y),{k:v*y for k,v in dx.items()}

def defaults():return {k:p['value'] for k,p in m['parameters'].items()}
def validate_param(p,x):
    if x is None:return
    v,d=ev(x,{})
    assert not d
    if p['type']=='integer':assert v.denominator==1
    dom=p['domain']
    assert dom['min'] is None or v>=q(dom['min'])
    assert dom['max'] is None or v<=q(dom['max'])
    assert dom['parity']=='any' or (p['type']=='integer' and int(v)%2=={'odd':1,'even':0}[dom['parity']])
    if p['role']=='code_distance':assert p['type']=='integer' and unit(p['unit'])== (F(1),{}) and q(dom['min'])>=1

def fails(call):
    try:call()
    except (AssertionError,ZeroDivisionError,KeyError,Unbound):return
    raise AssertionError('negative vector did not fail')
assert set(entry)=={'version','map','model'}
assert set(m)=={'schema','source','units','parameters','quantities','bindings','nodes','assumptions'}
assert m['schema']=='bennett.import-model/1'
assert set(m['source'])=={'format','version','media_type','bytes','sha256'}
assert hashlib.sha256(bytes.fromhex(m['source']['bytes'])).hexdigest()==m['source']['sha256']

# bennett-dialect-spec

The LLVM dialect contract of the Bennett ecosystem: the marker-function ABI (names, signatures, positive legality requirement), portable handle convention, outlining / budget / region well-formedness conventions, materialised-normalisation rules, the residual-poison validator, and the legality conformance corpus.

It also carries a reference semantics from marker programs to the ISA formal model, so a competing plugin can be conformance-tested at spec level.

Depends on: `bennett-isa-spec` (its reference semantics targets the ISA formal model).

Layout:

- `spec/`: normative text. [`Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md`](spec/Bennett-Dialect-Spec.md) is the current draft (version in its title; 0.2.12 at time of writing, accepted by the sol seat in round 11), `bennett_markers.h` the marker ABI header, `errata-0.2.10.md` binding interpretations of accepted 0.2.10. See [`spec/README.md`](spec/README.md) for the change rules.
- `corpus/legality/`: legality corpus.
- `validator/`: Rust crate `bennett-dialect-validator`.
- `scripts/`: the dialect checker (`dialect_check_v02.py`) and `pre-push`, which gates a clean worktree of the pushed commit (cargo test in `validator/` plus the checker).
- `emission/`, `docs/`: emission notes and audit/brief records.
- `SPEC_VERSIONS.toml`: ISA version this repo is graded against (a test pins its current value; see HANDOFF.md for what is current).

Current state and open consumer blockers: [HANDOFF.md](HANDOFF.md).
# Bennett LLVM Dialect — next draft — OUTLINE 0.3

**Date:** 2026-10-31. **Status:** proposed outline for maintainer rulings; no draft text and no ratification implied. Prepared as the design sub-agent. Accepted baseline: dialect **0.2.10**, ISA **0.8**. Candidate target: ISA **0.9 draft**, accepted by sol/codex seats, muse held, K64/K65 awaiting maintainer ratification. This outline follows the structure and ruling convention of `outline-0.2.md`, while preserving the accepted text's subsequent council repairs (notably Appendix A).

**Scope:** all 40 open children of `b-dialect-spec-8wf`, the three requested dependency beads bz6/cbe/0t9, nonblocking PT12 bead 0y2, and the additionally relevant open ISA-wording bead gwf: **45 item records, 22 decisions**. The epic itself remains the scope ledger. Other open corpus/tooling siblings are not implicitly completed by this design.

**Reading key.** DLT IDs name `../bennett-conformance/clauses/DLT-0.2.10.statements.md`; ISA IDs name `ISA-0.9-draft.statements.md` there. An unsuffixed section or paragraph is a clause dependency; `.S<n>` selects a statement. K/E numbers are ISA §0 change-row mnemonics, not dependency IDs. NS is `../bennett/docs/Bennett-NorthStar.md` iteration7, especially §3, §6.1 and the entire §8 ladder. Audit row IDs retain their original 0.1 meanings. “0.1 capability” below means the north-star capability the 0.1 narrowing removed or omitted, not a claim that restrictive 0.1 already implemented it. Every item distinguishes that history from the actual 0.2.10 state.

## Part A. Draft structure and release boundaries

Proposed section plan: retain §§1–25 and Appendices B/C; add an opening change ledger and dependency declarations; update §§3.4,5,6,8.4,9.5,11.5,17.5–17.6,19.9,23,25 for ISA 0.9; preserve the accepted normalisation/map unless an item explicitly changes it. Appendix A becomes a provenance ledger of departures from ratified outlines, retaining all accepted council repairs. There is no proposed change to marker ABI signatures or descriptor version2 in this outline.

Proposed editorial/process sentences (for the eventual draft, subject to D01):

- The next draft MUST preserve every north-star capability and every existing non-narrowing construction, with deferral limited to mechanics and any real missing ISA operation named precisely as a blocker. [deps: DLT-front-P3, DLT-1.3, DLT-1.6, DLT-24, DLT-25.1]
- Every new or changed normative sentence MUST end with a dependency declaration naming the DLT or ISA statement or clause IDs on which it relies. [deps: DLT-1.5, DLT-23]
- The 0.2.11 compatible track MUST retain the ISA 0.8 target, existing marker signatures, canonical map and externally visible reference behaviour while clarifying inherited rules and supplying their missing closure evidence. [deps: DLT-front-P2, DLT-3.2, DLT-16, DLT-23]
- The 0.3 track MUST declare ISA 0.9 and its complete sub-version tuple, regenerate affected objects, canonical hashes, plugin identity, module flag, schedules and certificates, and preserve the accepted 0.2.10 ISA 0.8 artefacts as historical versioned vectors. [deps: DLT-16.2-P2, DLT-19.9, DLT-23, ISA-front-P2, ISA-9.1, ISA-9.2, ISA-12.3]

The dependency-tag process rule additionally comes directly from repo CLAUDE.md rule12; its dependency marker above names the existing notation/versioning context, not an assertion that 0.2.10 already had rule12. Same principle for the no-narrowing standing rule (repo rule10, NS thesis).

Three groups below describe landing mechanics, never reduced scope:

- **0.2.11:** compatible/editorial confirmations and analysis clarifications already required by 0.2.10; these are mostly open-bead closure/evidence work, not newly invented capabilities.
- **0.3:** actual ISA 0.9 adoption, dynamic-register/token/R lowering and removal of obsolete dependency verdicts.
- **ISA-blocked:** bz6/cbe remain external adoption/implementation blockers in the accepted baseline, destined for 0.3 once the publication and execution gates pass; 0.9 contains their missing sentences already. This is not a claim of an additional ISA design gap.

## Part C — 0.2.11 item records

<a id="i01"></a>
### I01 — Taint-driven lifting

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.1`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D08. **Audit:** 1.4-d, D17, B-31, B-50, O-05.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 ran the classical part of ctrl/adj/compute once without controlling its escaping stores; the promised capability is inferred coherent execution of ordinary code.

**0.2.10 delta:** Already restored by DLT-7.1-P7 and DLT-10-P2 through DLT-10-P45: Cl/M/Co state, classical twins, coherent slices, store events and clean control workspace; no surviving generation-only restriction.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** ISA-4.7-P1, ISA-5.2, ISA-5.3 and ISA-6 provide controlled oracle calls, total XOR outcomes and cleanup; no extra ISA sentence is needed.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> An action body containing classical code MUST retain the Cl/M/Co partition, classical twins, predicated outliving stores and clean-workspace obligations of TDL-1 through TDL-12. [deps: DLT-10, DLT-18.2, ISA-4.7, ISA-5.2, ISA-6]

**Rejected alternatives:** Generation-only execution changes the controlled store result; retaining incidental garbage as undeclared outputs violates the accepted cleanup contract.

**Conformance consequence:** DT/DS: retain tdl-a through tdl-d, co-loop-identity, co-loop-adj-overwritten, co-observe-write-observe and deferred-channel cases; add no subset rejection. Rungs 4/7/8 retain explicit-oracle paths.

**Dependency order:** I07, I17, I22, I33; I31 is the nested reversible-call case.

**Maintainer choice:** D08 below applies to this item; the recommended resolution is the proposed sentence and retained construction above.

<a id="i03"></a>
### I03 — Parametrised inplace

**Bead:** `b-dialect-spec-8wf.3`. **Group:** 0.2.11. **Decision:** D14. **Audit:** 1.4-f / ISA-3.

**0.1 capability/history:** 0.1 inplace accepted only x, omitting preserved quantum inputs, memory and classical parameters.

**0.2.10 delta:** DLT-8.7-P3, DLT-8.7-P4, DLT-8.7-P5 already support all bindings by specialisation, library actions and the CC oracle/swap/inverse expansion; BLK-3 is retired in DLT-25.1-P1.

**ISA 0.9 provision:** K4: ISA-4.5-P5 now provides native qinplace with ins/mem/args and an inverse-pair entry; K62/63 pin adjoints and call-edge identities. This is an optional lowering, not a newly missing capability.

**Proposed normative resolution:**

> Parametrised in-place calls MUST preserve the existing CC expansion for every admitted pair. [deps: DLT-8.7-P5, DLT-8.7-P6, ISA-3.3-P9, ISA-4.7]

Optional 0.3 lowering, after D01:

> Native parametrised qinplace MAY replace that expansion only when its covering ISA inverse-pair entry is verified. [deps: DLT-8.7-P5, DLT-8.7-P6, ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-12.1-P1, ISA-12.3]

**Rejected alternatives:** Reinstating BLK-3 is false; replacing every pair with native qinplace would exclude the sampled path described in I14.

class Mismatch(d.Fail):
    pass


class Unavailable(d.Fail):
    def __init__(self, capability, command, bead):
        self.command, self.bead = command, bead
        super().__init__(f'P-2 checker-unimplemented: {capability}; expected executable check; '
                         f'actual unavailable; needs {command}; blocked-on {bead}')


def equal(example, rule, expected, actual):
    if expected != actual:
        raise Mismatch(f'{example} [{rule}] expected={expected!r}; actual={actual!r}')


def initialise(blocks):
    d.MARKERS.clear()
    d.MARKERS.update(d.marker_list(blocks))
    d.MARKERS_SHORT.clear()
    d.MARKERS_SHORT.update(n[10:] for n in d.MARKERS)
    d.DECLS_TEXT.clear()
    for name, fn in d.MARKERS.items():
        d.DECLS_TEXT[name] = 'declare ' + d.tstr(fn.ret) + ' @' + name + '(' + ', '.join(d.tstr(p[0]) for p in fn.params) + ')'
    d.DECLS_TEXT.update({
        '__bennett_nz_fault': 'declare void @__bennett_nz_fault(i64)',
        '__bennett_nz_alloc': 'declare ptr @__bennett_nz_alloc(i64)',
        '__bennett_nz_free': 'declare void @__bennett_nz_free(ptr)',
    })
    d.PERMITTED_FN.update('strictfp cold hot nomerge noinline convergent builtin nobuiltin optsize minsize optnone'.split())
    d.HANDLE_OPS.update({n: [] for n in d.MARKERS_SHORT if n.startswith('effect_')})
    d.HANDLE_OPS.update(qprep=[0], observe=[], oracle=[], inplace=[])
    d.ENV_OP.update(adj=2, compute=3)
    d.INT_CAND_OPS.update(inplace={}, oracle={})


def families(block):
    out = {TAG_FAMILY[k] for k in block['attrs'] if k in TAG_FAMILY}
    if block['lang'] == 'llvm':
        out.add('parse')
    if block['lang'] in ('c', 'rust', 'julia') and 'example' in block['attrs']:
        out.add('frontend')
    return sorted(out)


def headless_env():
    env = dict(os.environ)
    for key in ('DISPLAY', 'WAYLAND_DISPLAY', 'DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS', 'BROWSER'):
        env.pop(key, None)
    SCRATCH.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    env['TMPDIR'] = str(SCRATCH)
    return env


def command(argv, timeout=120, cwd=None):
    argv = list(map(str, argv))
    if any(re.search(r'(^-|[,(=])(?:-?view-)', a) for a in argv[1:] if a.startswith('-')):
        raise d.Fail('rule 11: forbidden LLVM view pass: ' + shlex.join(argv))
    try:
        p = subprocess.run(argv, capture_output=True, text=True, env=headless_env(),
                           timeout=timeout, cwd=cwd)
    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired) as e:
        raise d.Fail(f'command={shlex.join(argv)}; expected exit=0; actual={e}') from e
    if p.returncode:
        raise d.Fail(f'command={shlex.join(argv)}; expected exit=0; actual exit={p.returncode}\n'
                     + p.stderr.strip() + '\n' + p.stdout.strip())
    return p.stdout


def frontend_bins():
    import tomllib
    config = tomllib.loads(d.TOOLCHAINS.read_text())
    return {k: Path(config['llvm' + k].get('frontend_bin', config['llvm' + k]['bin']))
            for k in d.MAJORS}


def read_commit(repo):
    """Read the checkout identity without changing the checkout."""
    gd = repo / '.git'
    if gd.is_file():
        gd = (repo / gd.read_text().strip().split(': ', 1)[1]).resolve()
    head = (gd / 'HEAD').read_text().strip()
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Push gate (no CI). Runs the [[check]]s of gate.toml on the pushed sha in a clean detached
# worktree, with siblings at the shas pinned there. See bennett/tools/gate_README.md in the
# meta repo. The tool is run, never imported: it is a dev tool at a pinned commit, like cargo.
set -euo pipefail
unset GIT_DIR GIT_WORK_TREE GIT_INDEX_FILE DISPLAY WAYLAND_DISPLAY DBUS_SESSION_BUS_ADDRESS BROWSER
root="$(git -C "$(dirname "$(readlink -f "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")")" rev-parse --show-toplevel)"
if [ "${BENNETT_GATE_HOOK_PROBE:-}" = 1 ]; then echo "bennett-gate-hook-chained $root"; exit 0; fi
pin="$(git -C "$root" show HEAD:gate.toml 2>/dev/null)" || { echo "pre-push: HEAD has no gate.toml" >&2; exit 3; }
tool_sha="$(sed -n 's/^tool_sha = "\([0-9a-f]\{40\}\)"$/\1/p' <<<"$pin")"
[ -n "$tool_sha" ] || { echo "pre-push: gate.toml has no full tool_sha" >&2; exit 3; }
common="$(git -C "$root" rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-common-dir)"
meta="${BENNETT_GATE_WORKSPACE:-$(dirname "$(dirname "$common")")}/bennett"
tool="$root/.tmp/gate/tool/gate_run-$tool_sha.py"
if [ ! -s "$tool" ]; then
  mkdir -p "$(dirname "$tool")"
  git -C "$meta" show "$tool_sha:tools/gate_run.py" >"$tool.$$"
  mv "$tool.$$" "$tool"
fi
exec python3 -I "$tool" run --repo "$root" --tool-sha "$tool_sha" "$@"

## bennett-dialect-spec: resolve realloc size/liveness bookkeeping under exact C allocation history

Upcoming P1 brt-dj7 requires production realloc, not a native-only helper. The runtime C wrappers currently call only the three published nz primitives and track the next byte address. Live requested sizes exist in ISA/native allocation records, but the published ordinary-bitcode ABI has no allocation-size/liveness query. realloc must preserve min(old_size,new_size) bytes and reject dead/interior pointers, so it requires that information.

Determining boundary: section 17.1 and scripts/0212_allocation.c prescribe exact byte bumps, an ABI-rounded payload extent and explicit minimal padding; the 32-byte witness alloc(3), malloc(1) succeeds with requests 3,13,16 and exactly three records. Existing runtime tests enforce that same history/spacing. Adding a header to each C allocation violates the returned-base/rounded-extent boundary; allocating a side record for each malloc changes this success/budget/history witness. A fixed private record cap or native-only query is forbidden, and unconditional copying of the new size would fault on valid growth.

Please publish one concrete resolution: either a runtime-readable old-size/liveness mechanism lowered identically to ISA and native records, or an explicit allowance/ABI for charged C bookkeeping with updated normative allocation-history vectors. Clarify whether the simple C-wrapper trace is an exact production requirement or a minimum-payload witness permitting additional ordinary allocations. No allocator bookkeeping/history change has been made on an assumed interpretation. This is a contract decision for realloc; it does not narrow the requested libc/SQLite capability.

Realloc information witness: histories malloc(16), malloc(32) and malloc(32), malloc(16) have the same first pointer p and final next-byte address p+48. Their physical 48 payload bytes can be identical after valid writes to the respective blocks, yet realloc(p,48) must preserve only 16 bytes in the first history and 32 in the second. The wrapper's sole next-byte cursor and visible memory cannot distinguish them. The live allocation records can. Lazy bookkeeping beginning at the first realloc cannot recover these prior sizes; a resolution must cover earlier malloc/calloc/aligned allocation calls as well.

## Realloc implementation update: ordinary charged bookkeeping, no new query ABI

The earlier allocation-info clarification is not treated as a blocker. brt-dj7 now implements C requested-size/liveness state in ordinary bitcode: a linked list plus a pool of explicit 32-byte heap records, fully charged and visible in both builds. Freed payload addresses/history are never reused; live pool slots are reused only as bookkeeping data. Primitive and rounded payload/padding extents are unchanged; the minimal wrapper witness is not treated as a complete production allocator. Address/history fixtures assert the added requests and pool behavior exactly, and a store-history fixture reverses actual published allocator/list stores plus primitive history. No extra primitive, private ISA table access, host allocator, hidden size header, or fixed record cap is used. Update heap budgets/address expectations when re-pinning. This is an implementation decision within the ordinary-runtime-body contract; the earlier request matters only if the spec intends to prohibit explicit ordinary bookkeeping. Final verification/digests follow after the full gate.

# Bennett Virtual ISA — Specification, draft 0.8
### 7.1 Classical memory (CC, R)

Byte-addressed, little-endian, sparse. `layout` partitions the address
space: a **null region** `[0, null_size)` never allocated (null_size ≥
1); globals at fixed bases with images; the heap `[heap_base, heap_base +
heap_limit_max)`, all disjoint, `heap_base + heap_limit_max ≤ 2^ptr_
width`, and the number of globals ≤ 2^A_tab, all validated at load. `p =
alloc n` **fails before any mutation** when bump + max(n, 1) > heap_base
+ heap_limit_max or the table holds 2^A_tab entries (CC trap / R fault
`FAULT_BUDGET_HEAP`); otherwise it returns the bump pointer, records
`(base, size = n, live = 1, pinned = 0)` and advances by `max(n, 1)`;
globals have pre-entered pinned live entries; an access is valid iff its
whole byte range lies in one live entry; `free p` marks the unique live
entry with base p dead (pinned entries cannot be freed; no such entry:
CC trap / R fault `FAULT_UB_MEMFAULT`); storage is never reclaimed.
Initial state: bump = `heap_base`, table = the pre-entries in layout
order, memory zero except images.


### 9.1 Sections and schemas

Order fixed: `symbols imports functions data devices layout schedule
certificate coverage`; each at most once; unknown sections or fields are
errors. Metadata sections are one JCS line each, an object:

```
  symbols:  { "<local index>": { "name", "kind": "cc"|"r"|"oracle"|"action"|"perm_action"|"lproc"|"data", "signature": <sig>, "interface_hash" } }
  imports:  { "<name>": { "signature": <sig>, "pinned_closure_hash"?: hex } }
  devices:  { "models": { "<dev>": { "version": "0.9" } }, "services": { "<reverse-dns id>": { "version": "…" } } }
  layout:   { "ptr_width", "H", "A_tab", "S_cells", "null_size", "heap_base", "heap_limit_max",
              "globals": { "<name>": { "base", "size", "image": "<data name>" } },
              "interface_regions": { "<oracle>": { "<region>": { "base" } } }, "block_image"?: "<data name>" }
  schedule: §11      certificate: §12      coverage: { "entries": [ §13 ] }
  <sig>:    kind "oracle": { "tier":"q", "kind", "in":[w…], "tgt":w, "fault":F, "mem":[{"A","W","role"}], "args":[types], "abi":"0.9" }
            kind "action"/"perm_action": { "tier":"q", "kind", "mut":[w…], "in":[w…], "mem":[…], "args":[types], "bounds":[…], "abi" }
            kind "lproc": { "tier":"q", "kind", "params":[w…], "result":w, "tokens":["token"…], "args":[types], "mem":[{"A","W","role":"in"}], "abi" }
            kind "cc"/"r": { "tier", "kind", "params":[types], "results":[types], "abi" }
            kind "data": { "tier":"-", "kind", "size", "abi" }
```

Integers that may exceed 2^53 are decimal strings, and a JSON number ≥
2^53 anywhere in metadata is an error. `data` lines are `NAME SP HEX`.
**Encodings.** `types` in `args`, `params` and `results` are the §16.2
type spellings as JSON strings (`"i64"`, `"ptr"`, `"f64"`, `"token"`,
`"qreg[8]"`); `w` in `in`, `tgt`, `mut`, lproc `params` and `result` is
the integer bundle or location width (§16.3). An lproc's `tokens`,
`args` and `mem` arrays are mandatory (empty arrays when absent from the
header); `args` excludes tokens and `mem` has only `in` roles (§3.7).
Array order is header order within each group. A **program-point id**
(§9.2) is the JSON array `[interface_hash, block name, ordinal]`.
Certificate `frame` fields use exactly the typed-record schema of §9.4;
unknown fields are errors there as in every metadata record.
`devices.models` lists every §7.3 model that an `effect` of the object
uses (an `effect` on an unlisted §7.3 model is a load error; a device
outside §7.3 is never listed and faults at run time, §17 item 3; a key
that is not a §7.3 model is an unknown field); `devices.services` lists
every `hostcall` service (§10). An interface region's name (its param
name and its `layout.interface_regions.<oracle>` key) MUST differ from
every symbol and import name of the object, since `@name` in `lift.addr`
denotes either (§9.2).

### 9.2 Identity

An lproc's token, classical and region parameter groups are part of
its JCS signature (§9.1), including their order and types. The header
and every forwarded operand list are canonical function bytes; changing
them changes the envelope and, through references, the caller's closure
hash. Fixed binding instances are derived per oracle (§3.7), not new
symbols or separately hashed bodies; instance rows share the source
row's program-point id and expand once per fixed row instance per step,
while the instance and semantic-call occurrence components in §11
invocation paths and §12.1 context keys distinguish their uses. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11] Entry argument values and token tuples bind invocations through
`params_hash`, not through the lproc's interface hash (this section).
Imports must match all five groups; validation and instance formation
are repeated after linking (§9.3).

**Canonical function bytes**: the text from the function's header line
through its last instruction line, lines joined by LF, no trailing LF.
**Semantic envelope** of a symbol (byte-exact; every part ends with LF):

```
  (1) "isa=" <full version string, e.g. 0.9>
  (2) "ptr_width=" INT
  (3) the JCS signature
  (4) the canonical function bytes (with reference tokens replaced, below); for a data symbol: its data line NAME SP HEX
  (5a) for every data entry in the closure set, sorted by name bytewise:        "data " NAME " " HEX
  (5b) for every layout leaf in the closure set, sorted by JCS path bytes: "layout " <JCS key-component array> " " <JCS value>. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
  (5c) for an R/CC function, for every device model and service it uses, sorted: "device " <name> " " <JCS entry>
  (6) for every referenced symbol outside the symbol's SCC, in order of first reference: its 64-hex hash
```

**Closure set**, by symbol kind. **Oracle**: **all** `layout` leaves of
the object except `interface_regions` entries of other oracles (every
global's pre-entry affects its allocation faults), its own `interface_
regions.<oracle>.*` leaves, and every data image those leaves name.
**Lproc**: the leaves and images of every global `@g` it names in `lift.
addr`. **Action / permutation action**: no layout leaves; the `@image`
of each `qregion` is an ordinary symbol reference (replaced in part (4),
listed in part (6)). **CC / R function**: all `layout` leaves, every
data image they name, and in (5c) every device model and `hostcall`
service it uses. **Data symbol**: parts (5)–(6) are empty. A layout leaf
is a scalar or an object at the deepest level the schema lists (`globals.
<name>` and `interface_regions.<oracle>.<region>` are leaves; `ptr_
width`, `H`, … are leaves); a scalar's JCS value is its decimal text.
**Globals are data symbols**: every global `g` in `layout.globals` MUST
have `image` = `g` and the object MUST define a `data` symbol named `g`
of that size, so the token `@g` in `lift.addr` is both the global and a
symbol reference (replaced by the data symbol's hash; the global's
`base` and `size` enter through the layout leaf). An `@name` naming an
interface region (in `lift.addr`) is not a symbol reference and is left
unchanged. **Reference replacement**: in parts
(4)–(5) every `@name` symbol-reference token is replaced by `@` followed
by the referenced symbol's 64-hex hash (the symbol's own name in its
header line is a definition, not a reference, and is left unchanged); the **interface hash** uses
interface hashes of defined symbols and `SHA-256(name ‖ 0x00 ‖ JCS
signature)` (hex) for unresolved imports; the **closure hash** uses
closure hashes. **Acyclic and cyclic symbols.** The reference graph
has an edge from a symbol to every symbol it references (parts
(4)–(5)). An **acyclic** symbol — a one-member SCC that does not
reference itself — has hash = SHA-256 of its own envelope (example 1's
`inc8`, example 2's `zero` and `sum_db`); the SCC procedure below applies
to every **cyclic** SCC, including a one-member SCC whose function
references itself (example 2's `sum`). **SCCs**: members ordered by name bytewise; every intra-
SCC `@name` token (parts (4)–(5)) is replaced by `@$<decimal index>`;
intra-SCC references contribute no part-(6) line; `scc_hash` = SHA-256
over the concatenation of the members' envelopes in that order; member
hash = SHA-256 over the ASCII bytes of `<scc_hash hex>:<decimal index>`.
**object_hash** = SHA-256 of the canonical object text with the
`schedule`, `certificate` and `coverage` sections deleted together with
the blank line preceding each. `reference_program_hash` = the oracle's
interface hash. `params_hash` = SHA-256 of the JCS array of classical
parameter values (the typed encoding of §12.3, K13); `schedule_hash` =
SHA-256 of the schedule's JCS bytes.
**Program-point id** = `(symbol interface hash, block name, instruction
ordinal within the block counting nested lines in text order)`.
Executable certificates and caches bind to **closure hashes** (required,
§12).

A layout leaf's path is the JCS array of its original JSON key
components from the layout root to the deepest schema-listed leaf,
so a scalar has a one-component path and an interface-region leaf has
path `["interface_regions","<oracle>","<region>"]`.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
Part (5b) emits exactly one line for each structured leaf in the closure
set, including distinct leaves whose dotted renderings coincide, ordered
by the unsigned UTF-8 bytes of that JCS path array, independently of the
leaf values; no dotted rendering is used as a key or an ordering key.
[deps: ISA-9.1-P1, ISA-16.1]
An oracle's own interface-region leaves and entries of other oracles
are selected by equality of the original JSON key under
`interface_regions` to the oracle's name, never by a prefix of a
rendered path; an lproc's global leaves are selected by equality of
the key under `globals` to the global name.
[deps: ISA-3.7-P1, ISA-9.1-P1]

**Envelope boundary lemma** (non-normative).
For a function symbol, §16.2 requires a header beginning `fn `,
`oracle `, `action `, `perm_action ` or `lproc `; following lines are
`regs`, block labels, instructions, or structured closing braces. [deps: ISA-16.2]
No §4 opcode is `data`, `layout` or `device`; a result with one of
those names has ` = ` after the name, which cannot begin any part-(5)
line (a data name, a JSON array, or a device name is required there). [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
No canonical function line is a bare 64-hex string, so the first
part-(5)/(6) line ends the function bytes unambiguously. [deps: ISA-4.8, ISA-16.2]
The fixed `data `, `layout `, `device ` prefixes distinguish (5a–c),
and a bare 64-hex line distinguishes (6); JSON strings escape LF. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1]
For a data symbol, the signature kind selects exactly one data line
for (4), with (5)–(6) empty, even when the data name itself is `data`,
`layout` or `device`. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.2]
Together with the fixed first three lines and the injective JCS array
paths, these facts make the envelope parts recoverable without length
prefixes; this is an encoding argument, not a claim of SHA-256 injectivity. [deps: ISA-9.1, ISA-16.1, ISA-16.2, ISA-9.2-P4]

### 9.3 Linking

Imports resolve by name + signature (mismatch: link error; pinned hash:
equality required); resolved closure hashes are recorded in the linked
certificate. Equal-closure-hash duplicates deduplicate; unequal under one
name, or conflicting `devices`/`layout`/`data` entries: link error.
Linking = resolution, validation, certificate composition (§12.3),
rebinding to closure hashes.

### 9.4 Reversible ABI

- CC/R calls: classical frame; callee frames follow the call boundary;
  `uncall` reverses segments (§7.4).
- Region calls: interface bundles and regions passed in declared order,
  caller-owned; callee owns its workspace, returns it clean, local regions
  at image, borrowed bundles returned.
- **Frame descriptor** (certificate): interface slots retain their identities;
  scratch is recorded by class and cleanliness, with widths as expressions
  in inherited control count `c`; retained history is B × frame width for
  the full-history oracle expansion, otherwise the bound library's realised
  history (§17 item 18). Borrowed wires and scheme ancilla frames are
  separate records. Callees `qborrow` only from the caller's designated
  idle set. Shared frames require disjoint live ranges (suite F).

**Frame JSON schema (B5).** The descriptor is the following JCS object;
all five arrays are required, including empty arrays. Every record has
exactly the shown fields; `live_ranges` is optional. Unknown fields at
any depth are errors under §9.1.

```
  { "slots": [ { "id": "<slot identity>", "role": "in"|"tgt"|"fault"|"mut"|"mem_in"|"mem_mut", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "scratch": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "history": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "borrow": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "class": "<cls>", "width": expr }, … ],
    "ancillae": [ { "id": "<frame identity>", "scheme": "<scheme name>", "class": "<cls>", "state": "clean"|"dirty", "width": expr }, … ],
    "live_ranges"?: [ { "frame": "<frame identity>", "start": ordinal, "end": ordinal }, … ] }
```

`slots` lists the source interface in operand order: oracle `in`, `tgt`,
`fault`, then regions; action `mut`, `in`, then regions. Slot identities
are `<group>:<zero-based index within group>` (`in:0`, `tgt:0`,
`fault:0`, `mut:0`, `mem:0`, …); a region uses role `mem_in` or
`mem_mut`. A region slot's width counts all its cells, in address then
bit order; bundle bits are least-significant first. Slot widths MUST
match the signature. Inherited controls are caller-owned and precede
these slots in §11 topology placement; they are not renamed callee
slots. Every class is a resolved target class. Non-slot frame identities
are nonempty producer-chosen strings, distinct from every other record
identity in the descriptor; a dynamic identity additionally includes
the invocation context and the expansion scratch identity (§11.1).
Array order is source interface order for slots, and first allocation
order in the realised expansion for each other array. `scratch` records
per-class, per-cleanliness workspace; `history` records retained history;
`borrow` records dirty requirements; `ancillae` records each scheme's
ancilla frame and its declared state. These categories do not double
count the same wires. No requirements may be omitted because they vanish
for one chosen basis input.

Every `expr` uses §8.1's expression language with **only `c` bound**;
other variables and table lookups (whose indices require `k` or `eps`)
are invalid. At every covered inherited control count the widths MUST
be nonnegative integers; interface widths are positive and signature
fixed. Instantiation substitutes the resolved budget, parameters and
expansion choices into constants before forming these expressions.
Widths describe the actual realisation, including history and scheme
ancillae, and are checked against it. Each `live_ranges` entry names a
non-slot frame identity and a half-open interval `[start,end)` of its
invocation's generated leaf order (including step/phase/path expansion,

exec

### 12.1 Claims

| kind | payload | checker obligation |
|---|---|---|
| `exhaustive` | `domain` (§12.2); `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }` \| `"inverse_pair"` | re-execute every domain element on the reference interpreter / expected program; compare full tuples. For `"inverse_pair"` (the claim of an `inverse_pairs` entry, §12.3): for every element (x, ins, mem cells, args) run g and ginv at the entry's tuple, token-typed arguments at their `arg_tokens` tuples, and require both fault-free, ginv(g(x)) = x and g(ginv(x)) = x; the bundles and regions components MUST be the full domain of g's `in` and `mem` slots (the `args` component lists the covered classical values) |
| `sampled` | `domain`; `expected`: `"oracle"` \| `{ "table": [...] }` \| `{ "program": closure_hash }`; `subject` and invocation binding as below (K13); `sampler`: `{ "algorithm": "xoshiro256**", "seed": [4 decimal strings, not all zero], "count": n }` | independently replay both sides of the declared relation on every sampled element; compare full tuples; always **evidence**, even if every element is drawn |
| `structural` | `{ "rule": "translation_lemma" \| "inverse_pair_library" \| "adjoint_table", "subclaims": [indices], "witness": … }`; for `translation_lemma`: `{ "V": [points], "W": [points], "X": [wires], "B": [wires], "frame": [first point, last point] }`; for `inverse_pair_library`: `{ "library_closure_hash", "claim_index" }`; for `adjoint_table`: `{}` | check the rule's side conditions syntactically against the object and the subclaims' results |
| `numerical` | `{ "subject": { "closure_hash", "variant", "inherited_controls", "schedule_hash" }, "metric": "operator_norm", "precision_bits": n, "bound": decimal }` | **checker-computed, for every invocation context of the subject** (below): build the realised unitary U of that invocation (the region's realisation under the root schedule at that path, with its resolved classical arguments, budget, epsilon shares and cleanup choices, and `inherited_controls` control wires prepended) and the reference unitary V (the region's §5.3 semantics with the same arguments, controlled on the same wires) at the subject's signature widths in interval arithmetic at `precision_bits` (basis order = allocation order, controls first), form interval enclosures of `V†U − I` entrywise, derive the operator-norm bound as the Frobenius-norm upper bound in interval arithmetic, and verify it is ≤ `bound`; the claim holds iff it holds for every context |
| `channel` | `{ "subject": as above, "metric": "diamond", "precision_bits", "bound", "dual": [ { "Y": interval Hermitian matrix, "mu": decimal } … ] }` | for every invocation context of the subject, in enumeration order, verify its dual witness for the **diamond-norm SDP** below in interval arithmetic and `mu` ≤ `bound` (one witness per context; too few witnesses: reject) |

**CLAIM-REPLAY** (K13; b-isa-spec-9uy, from bennett-vm-clw).
Sampled observations remain evidence under the following replay contract.

**Comparison relation.** `exhaustive` and `sampled` claims
MUST name their `subject: { closure_hash, variant, inherited_controls,
schedule_hash? }` and an explicit `expected`; there is no implicit
self-comparison or default expected relation. Without a schedule the
left side is the subject's reference semantics at the bound invocation;
with one it is that invocation's realised operation. `expected: "oracle"`
compares the realised operation to §5.2's reference oracle map and
requires `schedule_hash`. [deps: ISA-5.2, ISA-11, ISA-12.1-P11] `expected: {program: H}` independently runs
the reference semantics of linked closure H at the **same** arguments,
budget and interface tuple; its signature MUST be compatible.
`expected: {table: rows}` compares to the explicit full-tuple rows
below. The checker MUST compare every interface output, preserve inputs
and borrowed systems, and verify clean workspace and exact +1 basis
phase for a permutation assertion (T3); matching a target or fault
projection alone, or a producer's claimed verdict, is insufficient. [deps: ISA-14.3-P3, ISA-5.6]
For a non-permutation realisation, use the existing numerical/channel
claims rather than labeling basis observations a permutation proof. [deps: ISA-12.1-P4, ISA-12.1-P5, ISA-14.2]
then Y′ ± J(Φ) ⪰ (δ − η±)I ≻ 0 and mu′·I − Tr_out(Y′) ⪰ (δ − η_s)I ≻ 0.
These margins make the matrices positive definite; they do not make the
interval test conclusive: every pivot enclosure the checker computes at
`precision_bits` MUST still have a strictly positive lower endpoint,
else the checker rejects (no fixed allowance such as 2^−`precision_bits`
bounds the interval error, which depends on d and conditioning). **Interval
arithmetic** at `precision_bits`
means every operation rounds outward to `precision_bits`-bit binary
rationals; transcendental entries (`rx ry rz`) are enclosed by a Taylor
series with the Lagrange remainder bound (§17 item 8 fixes the term
count). A `numerical` or `channel` subject's `schedule_hash` is required:
it names the realisation the claim is about. K13 also requires it for
`expected: "oracle"`; standalone reference relations use the explicit
invocation binding instead.

**Sampling.** For each domain component with range size R = hi − lo + 1,
assemble m = ⌈⌈log₂ R⌉/64⌉ consecutive generator words (least-significant
word first) into an integer, reduce modulo 2^⌈log₂ R⌉, reject and redraw
if ≥ R, add `lo`; components are consumed in enumeration order.

**Invocation identity witnesses (K63).** For `qinplace @G @G tk x`
at action A's `entry:0`, the two call edges below are distinct even with
identical arguments, and each prefix is also a round-trippable schedule
path selecting that invocation's descendants. [deps: ISA-4.5-P5, ISA-11-P5, ISA-11.1-P9, ISA-12.1-P11]
For two `lift.call` sites forwarding root token positions 0 and 1 into
lproc f, whose `entry:0` contains one `lift.oracle_call`, the next two
keys distinguish the row instances at outer step 1, even if token values
happen to agree. [deps: ISA-3.7-P5, ISA-3.7-P6, ISA-9.2-P1.S3, ISA-12.1-P11]
The `instance`/`call_index` fields are present in both schedule path
prefixes and context keys; context-only step/phase fields below are
supplied separately as §11 selectors in the schedule form. [deps: ISA-11-P3, ISA-11-P5, ISA-12.1-P11]

```json
{
  "qinplace_context_keys": [
    [
      {
        "symbol": "aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa",
        "point": "entry:0",
        "iteration": [],
        "instance": [],
        "call_index": 0
      }
    ],
    [
      {
        "symbol": "aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa",
        "point": "entry:0",
        "iteration": [],
        "instance": [],
        "call_index": 1
      }
    ]
  ],
  "lproc_context_keys": [
    [
      {
        "symbol": "cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc",
        "point": "entry:0",
        "iteration": [],
        "instance": [
          0
        ],
        "call_index": 0,
        "step": 1,
        "phase": "fwd"
      }
    ],
    [
      {
        "symbol": "cccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccccc",
        "point": "entry:0",
        "iteration": [],
        "instance": [
          1
        ],
        "call_index": 0,
        "step": 1,
        "phase": "fwd"
      }
    ]
  ]
}
```

**Expected-program correspondence (K73).** Compatibility for
`expected: {program: H}` requires the same ordered interface roles,
bundle widths/classes, region shapes/classes/roles, classical parameter
types and ordered borrowed-bundle widths/classes as the subject; local
names may differ. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-9.4-P2, ISA-12.1-P7.S4, ISA-12.1-P8]
The checker initialises and compares each interface bundle by (group,
slot), each region cell by (group, slot, index), each borrowed bundle by
its checked lifetime position and each prepended inherited control by
position, using the complete before/after tuples of K13, never matching
components between the two programs by name. [deps: ISA-5.1-P1, ISA-9.4-P2, ISA-12.1-P7.S4, ISA-12.1-P8]

*(non-normative)* For subject mut slots named t,u and expected mut slots
named u,t, both one bit, the first slot is compared with the first and
the second with the second; exchanging the expected local names cannot
exchange the values compared. [deps: ISA-9.4-P2, ISA-12.1-P7.S4, ISA-12.1-P8]

### 12.3 Schema and composition

```
  versions; bindings { object_hash, interface_hash, closure_hash (required), reference_program_hash, dialect_mapping_version, target_hash, schedule_hash?, invocation? (K13) }
  producer { name, version, materialisation { llvm_version, pipeline, normaliser_version } }
  regions { <closure_hash>: { kind, signature, bounds, estimate? (K12), frame (required typed-record descriptor, §9.4), resources { name: decimal }, gates_used { gate: { count, scheme?, epsilon? } },
            claims: [ <claim> … ], verification: { oracle: i, borrow_frames: [i], inverse_pairs: [ { g, ginv, claim: i, budget: [B,heap,stack,depth], arg_tokens?: { "<arg>": [B,heap,stack,depth] } } ], unitary: [i], channel: [i],
                                                   fault_rate: { domain, faults_by_code, undefined_excluded, layout_dependent_excluded } },
            cleanup: { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": [ { path, realisation } ] },
            error:   { "<variant>:<inherited_controls>": { local: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v }, inclusive: { "operator_norm"?: v, "diamond"?: v } } },
            definedness_assumptions: [ids] } }
  composition { multiplicities: { "<hash>:<variant>:<inherited_controls>": n (from the realised schedule) }, epsilon_total: { "diamond": v }, method: "sum_over_multiplicities" }
```

**Invocation binding (K13).** An invocation is
`{ args: [value, …], budget: [B,heap,stack,depth] }`, with every value
resolved before replay and all four budget components explicit even
when zero. `args` is in signature order: `iN` and `ptr` values are
canonical **unsigned decimal strings** of exactly that bit-vector
value (ptr has `ptr_width` bits); `f64` is `0x` followed by exactly 16
lowercase hex digits of its IEEE 754 binary64 bit pattern, most
significant nibble first (so −0 and NaN payloads are not lost); a token
is its four-component tuple of canonical nonnegative decimal strings,
not a process-local token id. Budget components are also canonical
nonnegative decimal strings. Signature types disambiguate encodings;
widths/ranges and every tuple MUST pass §5.4 validation. `params_hash`
is SHA-256 of the UTF-8 JCS **args array**, no trailing LF (§9.2), not
of the whole invocation. Thus equal token tuples have equal parameter
hashes regardless of ids, while +0 and −0 `f64` parameters differ.

Each standalone table/program/structural claim MUST carry its own
`invocation`; it binds
**every** classical argument and budget. Missing values have no implicit
zero/default. Claims with no arguments still bind `args: []` and their
budget. K4 inverse-pair claims instead bind the entry's budget,
`arg_tokens` and quantified `args` domain as already specified; this
rule does not replace that quantification by one chosen argument.
A parameter-uniform structural claim MAY instead bind
`invocation: { args: "all", budget: "all" }`: its checker MUST discharge
the rule's side conditions for every well-typed argument vector and
valid budget, not by replaying a chosen instance. Thus generic library
and adjoint/translation proofs retain their universal scope.
A scheduled certificate MUST carry the root `bindings.invocation`;
its args hash and budget MUST equal the root schedule's bindings, and
all nested arguments and budgets MUST be resolved at each realised
invocation context (§12.1), including default-covered contexts.
Explicit nested bindings, if supplied, MUST equal those resolutions;
a root binding MUST NOT be silently reused for a different callee.
A checker MUST reject absent or conflicting bindings before replay,
and an executor may use a verified result only for the invocation(s)
it actually covers. Reference table/program/structural claims need no
schedule; they prove only their reference relation, not a target
realisation. Scheduled/numerical/channel claims retain the expansion
library gate and all existing context and composition obligations.

**CLAIM-BINDING witness**

```json
{"signature":["i128","f64","token"],"args":["18446744073709551617","0x8000000000000000",["2","0","0","0"]],"params_hash":"9f628a694fa7259bf899bfb677aabce1c084e268a738a40f7f643eb6d8c9c093"}
```

The first argument is > 2^64; the second is binary64 negative zero;
the third is a token tuple, not an id. Replacing negative zero with
`0x0000000000000000` changes `params_hash`; replacing the token by
another valid id of the same tuple does not.

`versions` is the object `{ "isa", "object", "launch", "sched", "cert",
"coverage", "target" }` of the seven header versions. A **variant** is a
nonempty string chosen by the producer (the ISA enumerates none); it is
compared bytewise.

`inclusive(R, key) = local(R, key) + Σ_callees n_c × inclusive(c, key_c)`
**per metric**, keyed like `cleanup` by `<variant>:<inherited_controls>`
(the realised error depends on both); every `local` value is a verified
common upper bound over all invocation contexts of its key in the bound
root schedule (§12.1), so multiplying by n_c is sound; an
`operator_norm` value converts
to `diamond` by ×2 once, at the point it is first combined with a
`diamond` value; `operator_norm` conclusions (T6, controlled unitary
variants) require `operator_norm` bounds throughout. A controlled
unitary variant requires a `numerical` claim; an ε > 0 realisation
compared as a channel requires a `channel` claim. Executable claims
(`numerical`, `channel`, and `exhaustive` or `sampled` with
`expected: "oracle"`, plus table/program comparisons of a realised
operation) bind to `schedule_hash` through their subject. Standalone
reference relations have only the invocation scope defined above (K13).

Checker diagnostic codes other than codes explicitly mandated elsewhere
in this specification are implementation-defined. A checker SHOULD
document its stable code meanings and report the failed normative
obligation and certificate location with a rejection. Such codes are
scoped by checker name and version; their spelling does not alter the
verdict or any executor status/fault code. This convention does not
replace mandated E_SCHEDULE_INFEASIBLE behavior or K55 diagnostics.

## 13. Coverage (coverage 0.9)

`{ "entries": [ { "point": <program-point id>, "source": "<dialect-spec stable id>", "classification": "lowered" | "trapped" | "shimmed", "reason", "fault_code"?, "shim_hash"?, "count" } ] }`.
`fault_code` is present iff `classification` is `trapped`, `shim_hash`
iff it is `shimmed`.

## 14. Formal model

### 14.1 Reference interpreter *(normative)*

Executes an oracle's reference body per basis configuration: locations
and cells are bit-vectors of their declared widths (no upper bound,
§3.3), pseudo-locations read-only, predication is
`if`, each §4.2 row is its classical function (including the inherent
`inrange` check of loads and stores, the activation records of §3.7, the
`alloctab` and stack arrays), `fault` and a triggered `lift.faultif`
record their effective cause (§5.4) and halt, `lift.
retval` halts with success and `f(x) = result_val`, the step counter
counts executed rows (on budget exhaustion exactly B rows have
executed), counters are unbounded integers. It **defines** F_B
and the budget unit and rejects any body that executes a non-permutation
gate.

For CC/R, `fault C` has the terminal-record semantics of §4.9 and the
logical history of §7.4: it preserves the complete executed prefix for
eligible reversal, including activations that have not returned. This
is T4's history traversal, not the Q machine's B inverse steps. In an
